# Continuation implementation progress

## Composed project Canvas/Gadget/channel installer checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- Added an owner/admin project package install route for one versioned bundle containing CanvasMount HTML, project-bound Gadget databases, and external channel handler scripts. The route requires exact artifact-to-manifest path matching, immutable R2 release paths, source validation for channel handlers, supported provider declarations with required host-managed connection references, and explicit upgrade opt-in. It rejects package-declared permissions/capabilities, containers, daemon/workflow and command entries; it does not alter planner topology.
- Canvas and Gadget actors initialize under the existing project-bound MicroApp DO IDs. The first Canvas is saved into validated project `viewport_config` and mounted through the existing project-scoped Canvas route. MicroApp HTML is hashed when initialized and checked again before each render; tampering returns 503.
- Added an owner-review Shell form that shows the supported package boundary and requires review confirmation before installation, plus an API helper.
- Verification: Shell and Spaces TypeScript checks passed; 4 focused Spaces files / 16 tests passed (package/runtime install, project Gadget route, migration replay, Canvas artifact integrity); `git diff --check` passed.
- Remaining: provider connection requirements are declarations only and are not yet bound to per-installation OAuth/credential records; the current installer does not expose per-user or multiple named extension instances; Durable Workflow artifacts are not yet installed from a bundle; internal worker roles/tools are not invokable under package grants; extension release HMAC remains host acceptance evidence rather than publisher identity, and the Canvas byte hash is a DO integrity check rather than verification against the release record at every load. The Shell upload form is an admin-only package form and does not yet provide a complete review diff or installed mixed-package inventory. Continue with canonical artifact/release verification, then provider connection/instance lifecycle and workflow invocation without changing planner topology.

## Named extension instance checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- Migration 85 adds `instance_key` to extension installation records and replaces the old uniqueness constraint that forced one installation per extension/scope. Existing instances migrate as `default`; their deterministic IDs remain stable. A validated instance key participates in new installation IDs.
- The composed Canvas/Gadget installer accepts a stable instance key and derives separate MicroApp actor IDs for each Canvas/Gadget part, allowing independent state for multiple instances of one project extension. The channel-only installer and SDK identity helper preserve the default behavior.
- Verification: Agent SDK and Spaces type checks passed; Shell typecheck passed; Agent SDK identity tests 3/3; Spaces extension install + migration suites 18/18; `git diff --check` passed.
- Still open: each external channel binding currently selects one project channel installation, and `channel_sessions` remains keyed by `(provider, external contact)` rather than installation plus user/session. Therefore the new named-instance behavior currently isolates project Canvas/Gadget storage, but it does not yet provide multiple concurrently bound external bot instances or per-user multi-session routing. User-scoped external registration, credential/OAuth lifecycle, durable workflow deployment, and internal worker grants remain open.

Multiple named project extension installations and a many-session store now exist, but routing one provider identity concurrently to several bot instances and authenticated external-user identities needs an explicit host binding/session identity contract. Provider OAuth/credential bindings, worker/workflow composition, and cryptographic publisher/runtime release verification remain outstanding.

## Channel artifact release verification checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- The internal SpaceDO workflow-installation response now includes the active bundle release digest and the result of recomputing its HMAC attestation against the exact active manifest.
- Before a channel handler is loaded, Agents requires that attestation to be valid and recomputes the canonical sorted path/source digest over the complete immutable R2 package directory. Missing, extra, modified, or empty artifact sets fail closed. Per-handler script SHA verification remains in place after the package-set check.
- Verification: Spaces typecheck and extension release/install suite 9/9 passed; Agents typecheck and focused workflow/artifact, named-session, and handler compiler suites passed (13 tests); `git diff --check` passed.
- Limits at that checkpoint: the HMAC identifies host acceptance, not an FDE/developer publisher. Channel execution verifies the complete declared package artifact set at load. Canvas host-release binding was added in the Phase 5 checkpoint below; verifying every declared Canvas-package artifact from R2 on every render remains open. Workflow artifacts were subsequently accepted by the project package installer.

## Extension product lifecycle checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- Added SpaceDO migration 84 with immutable `extension_releases` records keyed by package/version. Every installed modular bundle records its manifest, aggregate source artifact SHA-256, installer, timestamp, and HMAC-SHA256 host attestation using `PLATFORM_SECRET`. The attestation establishes that this deployment accepted those exact recorded bytes; it does not assert a developer identity or replace an asymmetric marketplace signature.
- Added explicit monotonically newer bundle upgrades (`upgrade: true`) and rollback to a previously recorded release. Rollback recomputes and checks the host attestation before restoring the exact recorded manifest. Current channel installer forwards the digest of its sorted path/source map and exposes explicit upgrade confirmation; rollback has an owner/admin route.
- Verification: Spaces extension runtime install suite plus migration replay passed (9 tests); Spaces Worker typecheck passed.
- Remaining for this request: general install/consent UI for composed Canvas+Gadget+workflow bundles (the exposed upload/install route still deliberately accepts channel-only packages); deploy Canvas/Gadget artifacts and generate project viewport configuration; compose package workflow declarations into existing durable-workflow installation; richer provider-specific OAuth/credential lifecycle for each supported connector (existing OAuth flows and vault are present but not package-bound); verify release signature/digest at every runtime load and provide asymmetric publisher trust. No planner worker topology was changed. Exact next action: design and implement a package-level project installer that binds only supported Canvas/Gadget/Workflow primitives to the new project-scoped runtime surfaces, then add the corresponding owner review UI.
- Follow-up inbox slice: added an owner/admin-only project-installation inbox endpoint backed by existing channel message and dispatch rows. It filters by exact project and installation, reports queued/processing/sent/uncertain/failed outcomes, and has a bounded page size. Added a Shell view for recent customer events, replies, and handler errors, plus a focused SQLite test for isolation and dispatch status. Verification: inbox DO suite 2 tests passed, Agents typecheck passed, and Shell typecheck passed. The API requires owner/admin and does not expose internal planner or non-extension channel messages.
- Follow-up Canvas/Gadget slice: added project routes that verify project visibility through the owning SpaceDO ACL before routing into the project-bound MicroAppDO actor. Admin-only initialization accepts the project-bound scope and tiers 2/3; subsequent iframe, data, storage, and MCP calls resolve the same scoped actor. `CanvasMount` project views now use this project route, while legacy space-wide MicroApps stay on their existing route. Verification: Gadget route suite 5 tests passed; Spaces and Shell typechecks passed. The package installer still does not deploy arbitrary Canvas/Gadget artifacts or generate project viewport configuration, so this closes runtime isolation/mounting but not package-level install UX or review.
- Follow-up release management: added owner/admin release history queries that recompute the host HMAC validity and scope results to an active install in the selected project. Shell exposes verified historical versions and explicit rollback confirmation; the upgrade form includes an approval checkbox. Verification: Spaces extension runtime and Gadget route tests passed (13 total), Spaces typecheck passed, and Shell typecheck passed. Developer identity signatures and runtime verification remain open.

## FDE composition and mentor-bot architecture clarification (2026-10-17)

- The user's latest concern expands the target from an isolated channel handler to a coherent FDE/developer experience spanning execution tiers, workers/sub-agents, MicroApps/Canvas, Gadget state, internal tools/extensions, external channel handlers, knowledge uploads, provider setup, and optional session state.
- Audited `packages/agent-sdk/src/extensions.ts`, `packages/sdk/src/channel.ts`, `workers/agents/src/script-engine/execution-ladder.ts`, `workers/agents/src/script-engine/dynamic-code.ts`, `workers/spaces/src/do/MicroAppDO.ts`, project-scoped Knowledge routes, and the channel-extension dispatcher. The repository has the underlying components but not a canonical composed package/install contract.
- Concrete gaps: `@naelao/sdk/channel` models extension-owned `receive()`/`send()` while provider ingress is host-owned and runtime handlers are host-invoked; the two extension manifest shapes do not line up on Canvas; channel handlers have no explicitly published project-Knowledge grant or bounded external-subject session API; Gadget actor scopes are member/project based and one-object-per-external-contact would be an unsafe cardinality default; provider setup is host UI/configuration rather than a declared package dependency or common OAuth contract.
- Added `FDE-EXTENSIONS-AND-MICROAPPS-ARCHITECTURE.md` with a vocabulary matrix, actual-source discrepancies, a composed mentor-to-student path, principal/grant model, storage and runtime optimizations, SDK target and staged acceptance criteria. Updated the cross-source reconciliation, open-gap plan, and `FOUNDRY-REMAINING.md`. Source transcripts remain unchanged.
- Design disposition: an extension is a versioned composition; workers are invocation roles; MicroApps are UI/data surfaces; execution tiers are runtime/compute choices; connectors own provider auth/transport; external channel handlers use an external principal and explicit named data/action grants. These terms must not be used as mutually exclusive package types. This preserves the user's later external-extension correction and the existing planner-worker topology.
- No product code changed in the initial architecture checkpoint. `git diff --check` passed; tests/typechecks were not run. After the SDK correction below, the exact next implementation action is to reconcile the extension bundle/compiler/install record, then add project Knowledge publication grants and external-subject session storage before attempting the full mentor template/provider onboarding vertical slice. Provider OAuth must be implemented per supported provider, not assumed universal.
- Follow-up implementation: changed `@naelao/sdk/channel` so `ChannelAdapter` is declarative and `ChannelHandlerModule` types the host-invoked restricted `run(args, ctx)` entrypoint; removed the misleading extension-owned `receive()`/`send()` model. Updated the existing SDK contract fixture to use the actual channel-only contract (no permissions/capabilities, `message.received`, `message.reply`, and a valid handler path). `git diff --check` passed; tests/typechecks were not run. This fixes the public typing mismatch only: the versioned bundle compiler/installer, approved project Knowledge collection grant, public-subject session store, Canvas/Gadget composition, and host-managed provider setup remain open. No planner-worker topology changed.
- Composable package contract implementation: added `parseExtensionBundle` and declarations for independent worker invocation role/execution tier, host-managed connector requirements, and named member-only or owner-published Knowledge requirements. It validates primitive uniqueness/references, supported axis values, unknown-field rejection, and forbids package-supplied connector secrets. Added SDK contract test cases for composition and privilege-boundary rejections; tests and type checks were not run per session instructions. `git diff --check` passed. This is validation only: artifact bundling/signing, persistence/install binding, published Knowledge APIs, session storage, and UI/provider onboarding remain open. Exact next action: implement the backend owner-published project Knowledge collection and the channel-only bounded search grant, after tracing the current Knowledge ACL and D1 migration conventions.
- Product correction: removed `whatsapp-support-bot`, `discord-community-bot`, and `email-support-bot` from the active project-template catalog and removed their Launchpad SpaceType bootstrap references. Generic project presets and host-managed channel APIs remain. Updated admin-facing workspace guidance and marked appendix A1.13 historical/superseded so customer behavior is clearly extension-owned. Updated template and application tests. Verification: 4 focused template/application/bootstrap/workspace-admin test files passed (41 tests). The accompanying product-shell fixture failures were repaired in the next incremental change below; continue with the Knowledge publication grant.

Knowledge Worker APIs allow members to list publication metadata, owners/admins to publish/revoke collections containing only indexed documents in the exact project, and a service-only search endpoint to vector-filter by the exact published document IDs and recheck each returned chunk against the publication relation. Retrieved chunks are source-attributed and wrapped as untrusted. Tests cover authorization, foreign/unindexed document rejection, list metadata, and exact collection search. Not yet wired: extension manifest collection binding/owner grant, AgentDO calling the internal search endpoint, planner-upload reuse, and immediate end-to-end revoke behavior. ## Latest follow-up checkpoint — attachment understanding and container marketplace (2026-10-15)

- Implemented browser/chat upload classification using the existing task-scoped attachment index and installed-tool-aware System One choices. Classification remains metadata-only/advisory; image bytes are accessed only through a planner-selected operation and host authorization. Customer agents do not receive cross-customer copy capability.
- Added distinct `attachment.describe` for PNG/JPEG visual questions, using the already configured bounded image-query provider. It enforces owner opt-in, active Space, task ownership, egress/air-gap policy, size/pixel/question/output limits, and marks responses as untrusted. OCR remains a separate operation. Added customer-safe operation wiring and tests.
- Audio transcription remains bounded and task-scoped. Added an explicit video-audio path for MP4, WebM, QuickTime, and Matroska: a task-owned video (up to 6 MiB) is sent to the scoped, metered MediaSandbox, FFmpeg extracts mono 16 kHz Opus from at most 60 seconds, output is bounded to 1 MiB, then the existing Whisper adapter transcribes it. The System One classifier may recommend this only as audio transcription, never as visual video understanding. Container scope binds tenant, Space, task, and project; no provider credentials or general egress enter the sandbox. Generic audio/video model-provider dispatch and video frame/visual summaries remain unsupported.
- Discord media-only messages are recognized, but non-ephemeral CDN retrieval remains unavailable to Cloudflare Workers per the checked Discord Workers guide. No arbitrary-egress fallback or credential-bearing relay was introduced. An authorized private-machine relay would require a separately scoped grant and bounded binary transport; this requires an explicit product/authorization design before implementation.
- Implemented a Foundry runtime-card submission form that creates owner-review metadata only, and runtime dependency declarations on templates. Tests compare dependencies to the deployment-approved catalog, and installs require dependencies to be approved and already installed in the exact target scope. No user submission can execute/deploy an image; a platform operator must independently approve, build, and deploy an immutable digest. Project, Space Assistant, and isolated Space-extension workspace scopes continue to be separate.
- Verification after this checkpoint: Agents full suite 106 files / 430 tests passed; Agent SDK full suite 21 files / 85 tests passed; Agents, Spaces, Shell, and Agent SDK TypeScript checks passed; Spaces Foundry suite 17 tests passed. Local Docker integration extracted a 3-second fixture MP4's audio to a valid 14,761-byte Ogg/Opus stream. Agents Wrangler dry-run passed and built the Worker plus ToolSandbox, DocumentSandbox, and FFmpeg MediaSandbox images; Spaces Wrangler dry-run passed. Docker initially ran out of filesystem capacity during the dry-run; reclaimable build cache was cleared and packaging then passed. These are local/mocked checks; no provider transcription quality or Cloudflare deployment/live image startup was tested. The Spaces package has no `build` script; Wrangler dry-run is its packaging verification.
- Cloud onboarding setup was checked: existing checkout is `/workspace/Naelao-1` on `Mcbt5s-Backup-2`; Node 24.19.0 and pinned pnpm 9.12.0 frozen install succeeded after online retry because the FFmpeg dependency was absent from the offline package cache. Existing reusable environment setup draft was sufficient, so it was left unchanged. No repository dependency or lockfile changes were needed.
- Local Docker integration generated a short MP4 with an audio track and called the actual MediaSandbox harness. FFmpeg returned a valid Ogg/Opus payload (14,761 bytes); invalid/mismatched task scope is covered by the DO unit test. This proves local container behavior, not Cloudflare's deployed image startup or provider transcription quality.
- Current unresolved actions are generic model-provider dispatch beyond existing transcription, video-frame/visual summaries, a separately authorized private relay design if non-ephemeral Discord ingestion is required, live Cloudflare image acceptance, and platform-operated runtime publication/deployment. These are explicit partials, not silently completed items. Task 03 ledger now records the implemented proposal/review and template dependency behavior.

## Current follow-up checkpoint — chat/media classification and runtime placement (2026-10-15)

- The 33 USER/AI pairs (messages 1–66) remain individually tracked below; each was assessed in chronological order. This checkpoint covers the user's subsequent direction to finish general channel-media intake and a structured System One attachment classifier. It does not mark unrelated partial pairs complete.
- Added per-attachment System One output: one metadata-only choice can recommend `not_needed`, `inspect`, `copy`, `read`, `ocr`, `transcribe`, or `uncertain`, tied to a specific opaque attachment ID where applicable. The classifier filters choices against available tools and MIME compatibility; the recommendation is advisory and the host still enforces scope/authorization. Customer channel assistants receive only message-scoped list/inspect/read/OCR/transcription operations; project-wide copy remains withheld because those workspaces are shared across customers.
- Extended signed Meta WhatsApp intake to resolve image/document/audio/video/sticker media IDs through the existing per-Space or platform access-token path, and Twilio WhatsApp media URLs through the signed account/message resource. Telegram accepts media-only updates and resolves file IDs through `getFile` with the configured platform bot token. Slack now requests `files:read`, stores its bot OAuth token in the encrypted Space credential vault, resolves file IDs with `files.info`, and downloads only Slack-owned HTTPS URLs. Discord media-only events are recognized, but media download is restricted to ephemeral attachments from `cdn.discordapp.com`: the official Discord API guide for Cloudflare Workers documents non-ephemeral CDN fetches return 403. These provider paths enforce no redirects, bounded reads, provider size checks where available, a 5 MiB per-file / 15 MiB aggregate cap, safe filenames, and common task-scoped records.
- Common storage derives retry-stable opaque IDs with HMAC over channel and exact tenant/Space/message/index scope, uses deterministic private object keys, sanitizes filenames, rejects over-count/size input, and skips already-stored rows on webhook replay. Browser/chat upload requests pass only selected filenames; the server reconstructs keys from authenticated session/project scope and copies bounded bytes into task-private attachment storage so expiry cannot delete the original upload. Interactive runs perform the same metadata-only System One classification when the attachment CLI is available. Expiry sweep deletes generic channel attachment objects as well as legacy email attachment objects.
- Added task-scoped `attachment.transcribe` using the existing bounded Workers AI Whisper adapter for audio only; the operation is available to channel agents under customer-safe capability checks. Image OCR and supported text/PDF/DOCX reading remain separate. Video frame/audio extraction and visual image understanding are not claimed.

Latest full Agents Vitest run passed 105 files / 422 tests before the final per-file memory-bounded copy adjustment. After that adjustment, focused chat upload, channel-store, transcription and customer attachment suites passed 4 files / 10 tests, and Agents typecheck passed. Wrangler dry-run passed before that final helper-only refactor and built the Worker/configured container images. Checks are local/mocked; no Cloudflare deployment or account-authenticated download was performed.
- Documentation evidence: Telegram official Bot API `getFile` URL returned HTTP 200. Slack's official [`slack-api-specs`](https://github.com/slackapi/slack-api-specs/blob/bc08db49625630e3585bf2f1322128ea04f2a7f3/web-api/slack_web_openapi_v2.json) specifies that `files.info` requires `files:read` and returns `url_private_download`; current Slack docs host itself was blocked by CONNECT 403. - Started the follow-up plan in `ATTACHMENT-MULTIMODAL-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md`; this is an ongoing implementation, not a completed plan-only task.

The bytes remain in R2.
- Added a universal `attachments` prebuilt and virtual CLI commands `attachment list`, `attachment read <id>`, and `attachment ocr <id>`. The internal list route returns metadata only. Existing read/OCR endpoints resolve opaque IDs against exact tenant, Space, task and expiry before reading; legacy key-only endpoint requests remain accepted for compatibility but new model-visible messages do not contain those keys. OCR still requires owner enablement and allowed egress. Expiry cleanup removes the common metadata row with the old email row.

Running `pnpm --filter ... test` was blocked before test startup by repository supply-chain policy rejecting existing lockfile entry `qs@6.16.1`; direct installed Vitest runs bypassed no policy configuration and passed the tests. Full rerun including new tests remains pending.

- Added `workers/agents/src/media/modality-task.ts`: the normalized task boundary implements `transcribe` through the documented Workers AI Whisper audio-byte-array contract, with MIME, 8 MiB input, output, language-hint, timeout and cancellation validation. WhatsApp Meta and Twilio audio paths now call this adapter. Other declared tasks such as video summary reject as unsupported. Official docs: `https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/models/whisper/` (HTTP 200, input schema describes an array of unsigned 8-bit audio bytes). This validates only Workers AI Whisper transcription; it does not establish generic video, image understanding, arbitrary-provider, or live key entitlement support.
- Added metadata-only `attachment inspect <id>` route/CLI and route tests that assert tenant/Space/task/expiry scopes and that no object storage is touched. Current evidence after this follow-up: modality + WhatsApp + attachment CLI/index targeted suites pass (22 tests); rerun full combined suite/typecheck after next changes.
- Added `attachment copy <id> <workspace-path>` for storage-only intents. The command computes its destination under the active project workspace or the current Space extension's isolated workspace. The host resolves the opaque ID only for the exact task, rejects traversal/control paths and scope mismatches, uses a cross-run destination claim and R2 conditional CAS so it cannot overwrite a concurrently-created file, and copies the original bytes directly without decoding or returning them to the model. This is locally tested; no channel other than email currently produces common attachment records.
- Added scanned-PDF rasterization through `DOCUMENT_SANDBOX`: `Dockerfile.documents` pins the Node base digest and Poppler package version; the fixed renderer runs inside the DocumentSandbox Container with internet disabled by the existing DO policy. It accepts only bounded PDF bytes and a maximum five-page range, clamps scale to 2048 pixels, checks PNG dimensions/aggregate output, rejects encrypted/invalid/range-invalid files, and deletes scratch files in `finally` plus abandoned render directories at process restart. The OCR endpoint permits this only after exact task/tenant/Space attachment checks, active-Space and owner OCR-enable checks, and egress approval; it uses the existing per-Space OCR quota for each rendered page. Untrusted OCR remains labeled.
- Renderer evidence: `node --check container/server.mjs` passed; latest Wrangler `deploy --dry-run` built the Agents Worker bundle and all three container images; a manually built DocumentSandbox image ran locally under `docker --network none` and rasterized a synthetic one-page image-only PDF (PNG 1583x2048, 14,215 bytes). Malformed bytes returned 415, an invalid page range returned 422, a six-page request returned 400, scratch files were empty after the run, and a simulated container restart cleaned abandoned render scratch. This proves local container behavior, not Cloudflare deployment behavior or live OCR quality.
- Mocked OCR integration test confirms the exact task scope and calls OCR on the rendered page; the owner/egress and quota tests remain in the same route suite. Current tests are local/mocked unless explicitly called out as Docker integration above.
- Exact live checks still `unrun`: no D1 migration deployed, no Cloudflare container digest/binding acceptance, no paid Workers AI OCR call. Live credentials and deployment access are unnecessary for the local changes but required to mark rollout live-verified.

## Open-gap implementation workstream 6 addendum — resumable bulk archive rewrap (2026-10-15)

- Added SpaceDO migration v78 for owner/admin rewrap-job state, a fixed snapshot end cursor, progress counters, failure state, and a five-minute lease preventing concurrent workers from rewrapping the same batch. Starting is idempotent while a job is active/blocked. Each resume request processes at most three verified archives, invokes the existing per-archive verify-and-pointer-switch operation, and checkpoints the cursor/count after each archive. Failures pause at the failed archive for later retry; a restart safely resumes from stored progress. Completed jobs cannot be resumed.
- Exposed active/blocked jobs in the owner/admin data-ops response and added Start/Resume controls to Settings. UI confirms the operation, warns to retain the previous archive key until completion, processes bounded batches, and shows progress or a resumable pause.
- Updated the archive operations runbook and deployment acceptance log. Changed: SpaceDO migration and `spaceDataOps` actions, SQLite migration/restart/failure tests, Spaces data-ops API read shape, Shell API and Settings component/page, archive runbook, deployment acceptance log, implementation plan and this progress record.
- Verification: focused SQLite archive/migration suites passed — 2 files / 9 tests, including five-archive rotation, injected R2 write failure, blocked job status, DO recreation, continued batch processing, active-key envelope checks, and old-object cleanup. Full Spaces suite passed — 40 files / 116 tests. Spaces and Shell typechecks passed; Wrangler 4.149 dry-run passed at 12,058.16 KiB / 1,976.20 KiB gzip; `git diff --check` passed. These use R2 mocks; no live R2 check was performed.

## Task 06 — implemented for current project runtime

- Added virtual `lsp index [path ...]`, `lsp find-refs <name>`, `lsp find-symbol <name>`, `lsp get-signatures <path>` and `lsp status`. Calls require a project-bound run, pass the current tenant/project through the authenticated Agents worker route, read only the current R2 project workspace and respect delegated file scopes. Full refresh replaces stale entries; partial path indexing preserves other paths. Input is bounded to 200 files, 512KB per file and 2MB total. Files over 512KB are skipped only in full refresh and rejected when explicitly selected.
- Added a private project-SQLite identifier occurrence index that ignores ordinary line/block comments, quoted strings and Python triple-quoted strings. Occurrence search is lexical, not semantic: it does not resolve imports, scopes, properties or template-string expressions. Added FDE template guidance that prefers these commands but falls back to normal reads when the bounded index is unavailable/incomplete. No default-read capability was removed.
- Investigated the missing grammar artifact. `web-tree-sitter@0.27.1` with `tree-sitter-wasms@0.1.13` failed to load the published TypeScript grammar (`need dylink section`); the grammar loads under old `web-tree-sitter@0.20.8` in Node, but that older runtime and its filesystem-based init have not been verified in Cloudflare Workers. The all-language package is 51.8MB unpacked and third-party prebuilt grammar artifacts are not a safe production dependency without ABI, licensing and Worker-bundle validation. No package or binary was added. Exact remaining work: vet a current ABI-compatible TypeScript/JavaScript/Python grammar and compiled-WASM import with Wrangler/provider limits before replacing the lexical indexer; add installable manifest/template binding if the generic extension installer is introduced. No credential or human action is required.
- Verification: four targeted Agents test files passed (18 tests total), including a real SQLite index/query refresh, scope-aware virtual Bash request with a doubled worker transport, MCP size/dispatch behavior and CLI parsing. Agents typecheck passed. This does not prove live Worker or Cloudflare WASM behavior.

## Task 07 — implemented for explicitly trusted package hosts

- The user rejects a custom immutable caching/scanning registry and asks for trusted package sources. The prior model's claim that this Cloudflare binding offers kernel DNS allowlisting is unsupported. Current official Cloudflare Durable Object Container docs and installed Workers types expose `interceptOutboundHttps(host, Fetcher)`; an intercepted host can be forwarded directly while general container internet stays disabled. The Cloudflare docs say the runtime-managed interception CA must be trusted at `/etc/cloudflare/certs/cloudflare-containers-ca.crt`.
- Added `package-egress.ts` with exact HTTPS allowlist for npm, PyPI artifact downloads, crates.io and common GitHub source/release hosts. Cold starts register those hosts with native HTTPS interception before starting the container with `enableInternet:false`; redirects return to the container, where only separately intercepted targets can be reached. There is no app cache or malware scanner. The container startup script trusts the provider CA only when its documented runtime mount exists.
- The existing scoped mutation-grant sentry now requires approval for direct npm/pip/cargo dependency additions and `Edit`/`Write` changes to dependency manifests/lockfiles. Clean locked restores such as `npm ci` and `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` remain available. This is a deterministic command/file-tool guard, not a shell AST or complete defense against indirect package writes; host egress remains the independent boundary.
- Added `docs/trusted-package-egress.md` and tests. Verification: `packages/agent-sdk` write-guard suite 3 passed; Agents `package-egress`, `ToolSandboxDO`, `specialized-workspaces`, `execution-ladder` suites: 4 files / 32 tests passed; Agents and Agent SDK typechecks passed. Wrangler 4.151.0 dry-run built all three configured images after the final code edits. No live Cloudflare egress/container test was run. ## Follow-up implementation — task-scoped Cloudflare email replies (partial)

- Requirements reread in bounded windows: task31 primary 4983–5145; task32 amendment 5146–5259; task33 5260–5334. The reply's invented Cloudflare/MIME APIs and costs were not copied blindly. Official Cloudflare Email Routing docs describe a Worker `email()` entry point, distinct from the Email Service outbound binding. Customer-domain DNS and live delivery are still deployment prerequisites.
- Added optional `EMAIL_INGRESS_DOMAIN` plus the Agents Worker's Cloudflare `email()` handler. It looks up an opaque address digest in D1, checks active Space ownership and expiration, rejects unmatched routes, parses raw MIME with `postal-mime`, stores bounded attachments in R2 and sends the message through the existing per-agent email channel. Replies are associated with the initiating run id in `channel_messages.task_id`; routed worker and project scope are retained.
- Added `email.reply_address`: a stable HMAC-derived address per tenant/Space/task, with 30-day expiration and a cap of 100 active aliases per Space. The send route checks the alias belongs to the current task before forwarding `reply_to` through Notifications/Resend. AgentMail Reply-To semantics are not assumed. A five-minute sweep removes expired alias records and deletes attachment objects after 30 days.
- Added message bounds (20 MiB raw, at most 10 attachments, 5 MiB each / 15 MiB combined), filename sanitization, no-HTML-as-instructions parsing, and rejection on missing attachment storage. `docs/task-scoped-email-replies.md` documents the operator setup and the distinction between Email Routing and Email Service. The single platform ingress domain is configurable; automatic customer-owned domain onboarding is not implemented.
- Files changed: `infra/migrations/0093_email_reply_routes.sql`; `workers/agents/src/channels/cloudflare-email.ts` and tests; Agents `src/index.ts`, `src/do/NaelaoAgentDO.ts`, `src/script-engine/prebuilt/business-email.ts`, `wrangler.toml`, package manifest/lock; Notifications `src/index.ts`; channel/email route tests; `docs/task-scoped-email-replies.md`. One channel-wallet test fixture was updated to decode the Uint8Array writes its R2 CAS fixture receives and to mock the existing customer-telemetry route.
- Verification: Agents and Notifications typechecks passed. Agents MIME/prebuilt tests: 2 files / 7 passed. Business-email route, immediate-drain/task association, replay, and wallet-gate suites: 4 files / 30 passed. Notifications email/chat suite: 1 file / 4 passed. Agents Wrangler deploy dry-run passed and showed the Email binding/D1/R2/DO bindings. Node SQLite applied migration 0093 and verified both tables. `git diff --check` passed before checkpoint.
- Limits: no live Cloudflare Email Routing, DNS, Resend, AgentMail, or Email Service call ran. An operator must configure `EMAIL_INGRESS_DOMAIN`, the Cloudflare catch-all Email Routing Worker action, apply migration 0093, and verify the outbound sender domain. Attachments are persisted and referenced by R2 URI; the follow-up `email.attachment.read_text` operation now retrieves bounded UTF-8 text formats only. Binary attachment parsing remains unsupported. Inbound replies are attached to the correct worker inbox and task id; they do not reconstruct/resume the exact prior DAG execution state.

Cloudflare Email Service is preferred for outbound when its binding and verified sender domain exist. AgentMail remains an optional compatibility fallback. Existing per-task reply aliases and task-scoped attachment reads remain intact. Agent-owned inbox aliases accept text-only messages; attachments remain available through bounded task reply aliases. Air-gapped Spaces cannot provision Cloudflare/AgentMail email or send outbound email.

Activation is owner/admin-only, writes the exact Space model, and honors whitelist/air-gap policy. Product end users cannot override the activated model; inference still uses Space BYOK. Public catalog presence does not prove model entitlement, which remains provider-confirmed at inference time.

## Open-gap implementation workstream 2 — bounded email PDF extraction (2026-10-15)

- Re-inspected the task alias, R2 object, HMAC token, D1 row and expiry check. The route still checks the exact `(tenant_id, space_id, task_id, object_key, expires_at)` tuple before R2 access; that lookup remains ahead of all format checks and parsing. The prebuilt operation still requires the active task ID and only calls the internal route with its object key.
- Compared parser options with current official Cloudflare Workers limits and Node.js compatibility docs: [Workers limits](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/platform/limits/) and [Node.js compatibility](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/runtime-apis/nodejs/). The Worker is configured with `nodejs_compat`; a dry-run bundles below the 64 MiB uncompressed Workers limit. Cloudflare's platform CPU budget remains the hard CPU cutoff, so no uncancellable JavaScript `Promise.race` timeout is represented as CPU control.
- Initially chose `unpdf@1.8.1` (MIT, no runtime dependencies, serverless PDF.js bundle, Workers explicitly listed as supported) for PDFs. Its package bundles PDF.js under Apache-2.0; retained both MIT and Apache license texts and added third-party attribution. DOCX support was added in the W2 addendum below; other office formats and OCR remain unsupported, and no attachment content is uploaded to a provider.
- The extractor accepts at most 1 MiB of bytes, 10 pages and 64 KiB of extracted UTF-8 text. It checks PDF signature, disables XFA, disallows image rendering, reads text from a bounded stream, cancels when the output limit is reached, rejects password-protected/malformed PDFs, and never asks PDF.js to fetch links. Extracted text includes the original key and an explicit untrusted-data label. HTML and archives return unsupported-format status; existing text/CSV/Markdown/JSON/XML behavior remains at 64 KiB.
- Changed: `workers/agents/package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `src/channels/email-pdf.ts` and tests, encrypted PDF test fixture, task-scoped endpoint in `src/index.ts`, attachment operation description, `workers/agents/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`, MIT/Apache license texts, and `docs/task-scoped-email-replies.md`.
- Verification: parser, endpoint and prebuilt suites — 3 files / 14 tests passed, including actual PDF parsing, encrypted/malformed/oversize/page-limit cases, output-byte limit, cross-task D1 miss before R2, ZIP/HTML rejection and no network `fetch` during route extraction. Agents typecheck passed; `git diff --check` passed. Wrangler dry-run built the Worker at 17,825.38 KiB / 3,295.51 KiB gzip and exited without deployment or account access. PDF.js emits a benign missing standard-font-data warning for the synthetic Helvetica fixture; extracted content matched the fixture. This validates local parser behavior only, not adversarial PDF performance or live Cloudflare account execution.
- Status at initial PDF pass: IMPLEMENTED AND LOCALLY VERIFIED for bounded PDF and existing text reads. DOCX support and OCR status are updated in the addendum below. Parser CPU is constrained by Cloudflare's runtime CPU budget; no independent per-request interrupt was added. No operator action is needed for local PDF/DOCX work.
- Next action at initial PDF pass: workstream 3 — verify current provider APIs and repository bindings, then implement a provider-neutral customer-number lifecycle only where exact account contracts support it. Owner voice and customer telephony remain separate.

### Workstream 3 research checkpoint

- Current Cloudflare Voice docs now describe first-party server-side Twilio and Plivo media-stream adapters. This supersedes the older assumption that Cloudflare only exposed browser/WebSocket voice. `@cloudflare/voice-twilio@0.1.1` is MIT, published by Cloudflare on 2026-09-18, and its peer range includes the repository's `agents@0.28.1`. The official guide describes inbound TwiML/Media Streams, but the package's `handleRequest()` does not validate Twilio signatures, does not scope calls to Space/agent, and blindly forwards media frames; importing it unwrapped would violate the local acceptance/security boundary.
- Official Cloudflare docs also require telephony-compatible raw PCM; the current `WorkersAITTS` default MP3 cannot feed the adapter safely. Workers AI Aura model docs expose `encoding: linear16`, `container: none`, and configurable sample rate, so a narrow PCM TTS provider is possible without rebuilding the transport.
- Twilio's current `IncomingPhoneNumber` API officially supports listing, purchasing, updating `VoiceUrl` and releasing a number. Programmable Voice webhook signature documentation recommends Twilio's SDK validator and says parameters may evolve; standard Voice callbacks do not provide a signed timestamp field. Idempotency must therefore use provider CallSid, with Naelao's own short-lived timestamped single-use ticket for the media stream.
- Design constraint under investigation: Twilio `<Stream>` URLs do not accept query strings; custom `<Parameter>` values arrive inside the first WebSocket `start` event. The upstream Cloudflare Twilio adapter consumes that event internally and does not forward custom parameters to `VoiceAgent`. A safe integration must carry an opaque per-call ticket in the stream path (not query) and use the adapter's `instanceName` hook to deliver it to the scoped DO, while comparing the adapter's call identifier to the signed admission at a trusted boundary. Verify this behavior and tests before wiring ingress.
- Changed no feature code in this research checkpoint. Next: trace existing Channels/Space owner auth and D1/DO tables, and test whether a path-carried one-time ticket can be validated before Cloudflare's adapter forwards media.

### Workstream 2 addendum — bounded DOCX extraction (2026-10-15)

- Added task-scoped `.docx` extraction to the existing PDF/text attachment endpoint. The endpoint preserves the exact authorization-before-R2 boundary and recognizes only the official DOCX MIME type. It returns document XML text with the existing untrusted-data warning and original object key provenance.
- Added `fflate@0.8.3` (MIT, pinned by exact version and registry integrity) as a streaming ZIP reader. It counts archive members and declared expansion, starts decompression only for `word/document.xml`, caps input at 1 MiB, entries at 128, XML at 256 KiB, and extracted UTF-8 text at 64 KiB. Other members such as images and embedded documents are not decompressed; no relationships, URLs, macros, or external entities are followed. Encrypted/unsupported/malformed archives fail with a 422 parser status; archive and size limits return 413.
- Changed: `workers/agents/package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `workers/agents/FFLATE-MIT.txt`, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`, `src/channels/email-docx.ts` and tests, the task-scoped attachment route and route tests.
- Verification: focused DOCX/PDF/attachment route suites passed (3 files / 14 tests), including compressed ZIP fixtures, expanded XML and entry-count rejection, malformed XML/entities, task ownership, and no global network fetch during extraction. Agents typecheck passed; full Agents suite passed (86 files / 342 tests); Wrangler dry-run succeeded at 17,942.70 KiB / 3,321.99 KiB gzip; `git diff --check` passed. Package registry integrity was verified from npm metadata. The preinstalled `node_modules` links to pnpm 11's store while the repository pins pnpm 9; pnpm 9 refused to mutate dependencies with `ERR_PNPM_UNEXPECTED_STORE`, and pnpm 11's release-age policy rejected an unrelated pre-existing `qs@6.16.1` lock entry. Dependency/lock entries were therefore updated explicitly and the pinned package tarball was used for local checks; the release-age policy was not weakened or bypassed.
- Status: PARTIAL / LOCALLY VERIFIED for PDF, DOCX and existing text. OCR and a placement-aware provider contract remain open; no OCR content is sent to a model.

## Open-gap implementation workstream 3 — customer telephony provisioning and ingress (2026-10-15)

- Implemented an owner/admin-only Twilio credential vault route, Space voice policy route, existing-number import/bind route, safe number listing, explicit disable/restore, and confirmed release. Import verifies the Twilio account owns the number, confirms voice capability and USD number/inbound prices through Twilio REST APIs, saves the previous Voice URL/method and status callback settings, and restores them if local persistence fails. Credentials are stored via the existing scoped Credentials Worker contract and never returned.
- Added signed Twilio inbound answer, consent, media-stream and status callback ingress. Form and Media Stream signatures use Twilio's official SDK validator. `CallSid` is idempotency key; a short-lived Naelao HMAC ticket binds tenant, Space, binding, agent, caller/called numbers and expiry. Consent must be a signed DTMF 1 response before streaming. The signed ticket travels in the TwiML stream URL path because Twilio `<Stream>` rejects query strings; Naelao validates signature/ticket/admission before calling Cloudflare's `@cloudflare/voice-twilio` adapter and uses CallSid as its Agent DO name. This wrapper is required because the upstream adapter does not validate Twilio signatures or Naelao tenant scope itself.
- Added a separate least-privilege customer VoiceAgent with no planner or owner tools, bounded turn history, 140-token model output, 600-character spoken reply, 16 kHz linear PCM Aura TTS, and active Air-Gap/egress checks for admission, inference and synthesis. Calls disclose AI processing and no recording, are not recorded, and delete the adapter's retained voice message history at end. Customer phone numbers are masked before entering the visible Space voice thread; call transcript remains empty. Customer calls appear as non-internal voice channel threads; owner browser planner voice remains distinct.
- Per-Space/per-number concurrent-call limits, per-Space hourly and per-caller abuse limits, per-Space daily reserved duration, max call duration, and monthly spend admission are enforced atomically before consent/stream. Monthly spend reserves the maximum inbound Twilio minute cost and number monthly quote, and settles the signed completion callback by duration. Calls without consent release reservations; expired unconsented attempts are cleaned on the next admission. AI work is bounded by duration/turn/token limits, but Workers AI account billing has no verified dollar reservation here; the configured monthly amount currently covers Twilio number and inbound-call charges only.
- Changed: `workers/agents/src/index.ts`, `workers/agents/src/voice/customer-telephony.ts` and tests, `workers/agents/src/voice/customer-voice-agent.ts`, Agents `wrangler.toml`/`package.json` and lockfile; migration `infra/migrations/0097_customer_voice_telephony.sql`; SpaceDO migration v71 and customer-call session implementation/tests; HMAC-protected Space bridge and test; migration tests; this plan and progress record.

Agents Wrangler `deploy --dry-run` passed at 17,890.46 KiB / 3,309.37 KiB gzip. No live Cloudflare deployment, Twilio credential validation, number provisioning, pricing lookup or PSTN call was run.

- Re-audited model selection and policy in `workers/agents/src/index.ts`, Space BYOK resolution and failover construction in `NaelaoAgentDO.ts`/`shared/ai-client.ts`, catalog parsing/recommendations, local-model route constraints, air-gap policy, and actual token settlement in billing-core. Space-key validation and candidate-price validation are repeated in the Durable Object immediately before inference. Provider failure remains visible, and entitlement is never inferred from catalog presence. After an actual successful user-requested inference with nonzero reported usage, an atomic tenant/Space/model-guarded JSON update records the actor, run, timestamp, token counts and successful entitlement evidence; it stores no prompt/output/key.
- Dynamic OpenRouter post-run credit settlement now uses fresh per-token catalog prices through a bounded billing-core helper instead of silently falling back to Claude rates. Channel replies use the same exact price basis. Activation records owner/admin actor and catalog evidence while explicitly retaining `providerEntitlement: unverified_until_user_requested_inference` until a successful call. No hidden inference probe was introduced.

## Open-gap implementation workstream 5 — private adapter consent and lifecycle (2026-10-15)

- Verified Cloudflare Workers AI fine-tuning/adapter documentation remains inaccessible through the environment proxy (both current official docs paths returned HTTP 403). No supported training submission endpoint or live base-model/adapter compatibility contract could be established. No vendor training endpoint was invented; training requests now return an explicit `training_unavailable` response.
- Added the Space-scoped lifecycle `draft → consented → imported → evaluated/evaluation_failed → active`, with revocation, rollback, and deletion. Consent requires a lawful-basis declaration, affirmative consent, same-Space-only data, secret/protected-content exclusions, and an agreed deletion plan. Adapter import requires an allowlisted Workers AI base model and owner-supplied provider evidence; it is explicitly marked unverified. Evaluation requires matching base model, versioned test-set ID, bounded sample count, score, threshold, and optional non-regression baseline. Only a passing evaluation plus owner approval makes the adapter available to inference.
- Added migration 72 and lifecycle event records. Existing manifests are preserved as active `legacy_unverified` to avoid silently removing previously configured behavior; new manifests cannot bypass the new lifecycle. Revoke/delete removes the provider adapter ID from the inference manifest; delete also clears lifecycle provenance/evaluation details while retaining a minimal event. Rollback restores the prior active adapter when available. A custom model can be selected in Space model config only after lifecycle state is active, and air-gap/model-policy gates remain in effect.
- Hardened adapter-ID visibility: owner/admin lifecycle APIs return metadata without the raw adapter ID; the resolver path is protected by internal HMAC at both the Spaces worker and SpaceDO. The legacy direct manifest PUT now returns 410 and points to lifecycle actions.
- Added owner/admin Settings controls in `apps/shell` for lifecycle lookup, consent/provenance attestations, importing an existing adapter, recording external evaluation, approval, rollback, revocation, deletion, and selecting an active adapter as the Space default. The existing model-config route verifies the adapter is active and blocks selection under air-gap policy. UI text makes clear that training is disabled and evaluation values are owner-attested, not platform-run. Lifecycle reads do not expose adapter IDs.
- Changed: `workers/spaces/src/do/SpaceDO.ts`, `src/index.ts`, new `src/do/custom-model-lifecycle.ts` and tests, migration replay test, Agents `src/do/NaelaoAgentDO.ts`, `apps/shell/src/lib/api.ts`, `apps/shell/src/app/p/[slug]/(shell)/settings/page.tsx`, `docs/private-model-adapter-lifecycle.md`, this plan and this progress file.

SQLite-backed tests cover failed threshold denial, matching-base evaluation, activation, old-adapter rollback, revocation/deletion denial, training disablement, cross-Space state, replay-safe migration, and legacy manifest seeding. No live Workers AI compatibility call was made or claimed.

## Open-gap implementation workstream 6 — SQLite retention, encrypted archive, and restore (2026-10-15)

- Replayed all current SpaceDO migrations to inventory the final schema, documented every surviving table by ownership category in `docs/space-data-retention-and-backup.md`, and excluded migration shadows, `_migrations`, FTS indexes, and data-operations metadata from snapshots. Only old `messages` are eligible for automated deletion; session shells and financial, billing, security, legal/audit, provider, project, and other product state retain their existing lifecycle.
- Added migration 73 for owner retention policies, measured storage history, archive catalog, expiring one-use selection/restore previews, restore history, and append-only retention events. Added migration 74 for an explicit session retention hold. Owner/admin APIs use existing business membership checks, verify Space/tenant ownership in D1, derive actor identity from authenticated claims, then forward via a tenant-scoped internal token verified again by SpaceDO.
- Added per-Space message-retention choices, configurable byte warning thresholds, measured `page_count × page_size` forecasts, session hold set/release, and preview counts/serialized bytes. Apply re-queries and fingerprints the selection, rejects expired/replayed/stale previews, rechecks that any required archive exists in R2 and decrypts with a matching checksum/fingerprint, then atomically removes only those messages. Held messages are explicitly excluded. No hard deletion occurs when archive verification fails.
- Added versioned AES-GCM/HKDF encrypted snapshots with tenant/Space-bound AAD, checksums, migration version, row counts, scoped R2 object references, and stable request IDs. Failed R2 writes remain retryable under the same archive ID. Full snapshots enforce 8 MiB plaintext, 10,000 rows/table and 5,000 R2 references. Restore preview verifies scope, checksum, current schema compatibility and referenced R2 objects. Restore applies missing rows in an FK-aware order under one transaction, defers cyclic constraints, and rolls back on failure; it is explicitly a merge, not a destructive point-in-time rollback. Current SQLite remains canonical.
- `SPACE_ARCHIVE_KEY` can separate archive encryption from `PLATFORM_SECRET`; at initial assessment the key had to remain stable and whole bounded archives restarted on retry. The W6 addendum below implements a configured previous-key ring and resumable multipart uploads. Online key rewrap, backups over the fixed cap, and live R2 durability/cross-deployment restore remain open; live account checks belong to workstream 7.
- Changed: `workers/spaces/src/do/SpaceDO.ts`, `workers/spaces/src/do/ProductUserDO.ts`, `src/index.ts`, `src/do/SpaceDO-migrations.test.ts`, new `src/do/ProductUserDO-credit-reservations.test.ts`, new `src/do/space-backup.ts` and tests, new `src/do/space-retention.ts` and tests, new `src/do/space-data-ops.test.ts`, `docs/space-data-retention-and-backup.md`, `OPEN-GAPS-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md`, and this file.
- Verification: Spaces TypeScript check passed; final full Spaces suite passed (37 files/109 tests); focused SQLite retention/archive/restart/failure/migration tests passed (4 files/11 tests); local D1 migrations 0093–0098 integration test passed (1 test); final Wrangler Spaces deployment dry-run passed (12,011.87 KiB total / 1,968.75 KiB gzip). The SQLite shim plus R2 mock prove local behavior only. Package-manager installation initially hit minimum-release-age and ignored-build-policy checks; installed dependencies with a one-command release-age override, restored the original lockfile/workspace config, and ran tools directly. No live Cloudflare request was made.
- **Status: PARTIAL, locally verified** for preview/hold/confirm deletion, archive-before-delete enforcement, authenticated archive/restore, schema and R2-reference checks, transaction rollback semantics, retry identity, and (per the addendum below) key rotation/read compatibility and multipart resumption. Live acceptance and online key rewrap remain open.

## Open-gap implementation workstream 5 addendum — encrypted evaluation test-set vault (2026-10-15)

- Added SpaceDO migration v76 for encrypted test-set rows, keyed by Space/model/test-set ID. API access is owner/admin-only at the Spaces Worker route and signed internally to the SpaceDO; actor IDs for writes come from verified request claims, not the browser body.
- Test-set JSON is AES-GCM encrypted before Durable Object storage using an HKDF-derived tenant/Space key and AAD that binds tenant, Space, set ID, version, and format. Bounds: 20 examples, 4 KiB per input/expected field, 64 KiB total. API supports metadata listing, authenticated plaintext retrieval, save, and delete. Adapter deletion removes stored ciphertext; lifecycle events retain only IDs/version/counts.
- Evaluation recording now requires an imported adapter and a saved test-set version whose full case count exactly matches the recorded sample count. This prevents a passing owner-attested record from naming a nonexistent version or smaller sample. It does not execute model inference or verify scores; the UI labels the record owner-attested and clarifies that saving a set is not execution.
- Added Settings controls and Shell API wrappers for saving/deleting JSON test sets. Existing score, baseline, and threshold remain owner-attested because no verified provider adapter execution contract is configured.
- Changed: migration v76 and custom-model vault module/tests; SpaceDO vault endpoints, evaluation matching, and deletion behavior; Spaces Worker authenticated vault routes; Shell API and Settings controls; migration/lifecycle tests; this progress file and `OPEN-GAPS-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md`.

Tests verify ciphertext hides prompt text, wrong tenant/version AAD rejects decryption, input bounds, version/count matching, and cleanup. Provider scoring and live Cloudflare execution were not tested.

## Open-gap implementation workstream 6 addendum — archive key rotation and resumable multipart upload (2026-10-15)

- Added `SPACE_ARCHIVE_KEY_ID`, `SPACE_ARCHIVE_KEY_PREVIOUS`, and `SPACE_ARCHIVE_KEY_PREVIOUS_ID` bindings. Archive envelopes now authenticate a version label and use a keyring for decrypt. Because older envelopes used one fixed label for either `PLATFORM_SECRET` or `SPACE_ARCHIVE_KEY`, the bounded legacy lookup tries configured active/previous/platform candidates. The active and previous key material must each be at least 32 characters; invalid key version labels fail closed. Added owner/admin `rewrap_archive`: write and verify a temporary new-key copy, switch the record pointer transactionally, then delete the old object. The operation is per archive; no bulk rewrap job exists.
- Added SpaceDO migration v77 for an encrypted upload snapshot, multipart upload ID, completed part ETags, checksum, and archive metadata. The exporter persists a stable ciphertext snapshot before upload, sends R2 multipart chunks in 5 MiB pieces, checkpoints after every part, and resumes missing parts after a DO restart with the same request ID. Small archives use atomic `put` while retaining bytes for an identical retry. A completed object whose final local status update was interrupted is decrypted and verified before the record is repaired. Only completed, validated objects become `verified`; transient rows are deleted on success and excluded from backup/restore scope.
- Added mocked multipart restart/failure tests proving part 1 is not uploaded again after part 2 fails, the encrypted snapshot remains stable, and status becomes verified only after completion. Added key-rotation tests for legacy and current keys, and updated the operational runbook for old-key retention.
- Checked the R2 multipart implementation against Cloudflare's official multipart objects documentation (`https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/objects/multipart-objects/`) and the installed Workers runtime type definitions for create/resume/upload/complete; chunk size is 5 MiB as documented.
- Changed: archive keyring/crypto and multipart uploader helpers; SpaceDO env bindings, migration v77, checkpoint/resume/recovery flow, and archive scope exclusions; data-ops restart/multipart tests and migration test; `docs/space-data-retention-and-backup.md`, deployment acceptance log, implementation plan, and this progress file.
- Verification: focused archive codec/data-ops/migration tests — 3 files / 12 tests passed, including failure/restart on multipart part 2, reuse of part 1 checkpoint, both legacy-key-source rotations, individual key rewrap, archive replay, and migration v77. Full Spaces suite passed — 40 files / 115 tests. Spaces TypeScript check passed. Final Wrangler Spaces dry-run passed (12,051.43 KiB / 1,975.10 KiB gzip) and exited before deployment. No live R2 call was made.
- Status: key ring, per-archive rewrap, and resumable multipart upload IMPLEMENTED AND LOCALLY VERIFIED. Bulk rewrap and live R2 durability remain open.

## Open-gap implementation workstream 3 addendum — stale customer-call recovery (2026-10-15)

- Fixed call admission accounting so a `connected` call continues to occupy Space/number concurrency after the 60-second consent-ticket expiry; only an unconsented `issued` ticket can age out of the active count. Signed Twilio completion handling now clears reserved amounts and conditionally settles only an active row, making simultaneous callback/reconciliation updates idempotent.
- Added a bounded per-Space reconciliation helper. It releases expired unconsented admissions, then checks at most ten connected calls older than their maximum duration plus 90 seconds against the same account's Twilio Call resource. It validates CallSid/account scope, uses an eight-second timeout, disables redirects, caps response bodies at 16 KiB, and changes accounting only after a signed-in-account response names the exact call and reports a terminal status. A missing credential, provider failure, active call, malformed response, or missing duration leaves reservation intact for retry.
- Added active voice-number Spaces and outstanding issued/connected admissions to the hourly heartbeat roster so recovery runs even when a Space has no chat channel or the number was disabled/released mid-call. Air-gap/egress-denied Spaces still make no provider request.
- Verified the recovery contract against Twilio's current official [Call resource documentation](https://www.twilio.com/docs/voice/api/call-resource): the resource supports retrieving completed calls and the documented response includes call SID, status, and duration. Documentation was fetched read-only; no live Twilio API call was made.
- Changed: Agents `src/voice/customer-telephony.ts`, new `src/voice/call-reconciliation.ts` and tests, `src/voice/customer-telephony.test.ts`, `src/index.ts`, `new plan/OPEN-GAPS-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md`, this file.
- Verification: Agents typecheck passed; admission SQL replayed against SQLite and confirmed a connected call blocks a second call after ticket expiry; reconciliation tests cover terminal success, active-call retention, account mismatch, real SQLite schema/query/settlement, and voice-only heartbeat roster (4 reconciliation tests); customer telephony admission/signature/concurrency/roster tests passed (8 tests). Full Agents suite passed (89 files / 359 tests); fresh Wrangler 4.149 dry-run exited successfully; `git diff --check` passed. Twilio requests were mocked; no live provider test was made.
- Status: LOCALLY VERIFIED. Paid number purchase and live Twilio/PSTN acceptance remain open; account-specific AI dollar reservations remain unverified.

## Open-gap implementation workstream 2 addendum — explicit image OCR (2026-10-15)

- Added a separate `email.attachment.ocr_image` tool; the existing local PDF/DOCX/text reader does not automatically invoke OCR. A business owner/admin must separately opt in from Settings after a confirmation describing hosted processing, account billing, image/token limits, daily quota, accuracy limitations, and unsupported scanned PDFs. Settings links the model's license page. Enabling is rejected when Space egress policy is air-gapped/denied.
- The internal HMAC route checks active Space, owner opt-in, egress permission, exact `(tenant, Space, task, key, expiry)` attachment ownership, MIME, 1 MiB bytes, and PNG/JPEG header dimensions capped at 20 megapixels before inference. It uses a fixed Workers AI OCR model, 1,024 output-token cap and 12-second abort; no provider fallback exists. Returned text is marked untrusted. Neither image nor OCR output is persisted. Migration 0100 stores only random attempt ID, tenant, Space, and timestamp for a race-safe 20-attempt rolling daily cap; rows expire after 30 days.
- Checked Cloudflare's current [Moondream 3.1 Workers AI model page](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/models/moondream3.1-9B-A2B/). It documents OCR/image-to-text, base64 data-URI image input, `query`/`question`, `answer`, `max_tokens`, and current per-million-token prices of $0.30 input / $1.00 output. Exact image token use is not exposed before inference, so no per-image quote is claimed. The linked Moondream license endpoint returned HTTP 403 through this environment proxy; the owner UI links the terms for review before enabling. Model quality and live binding behavior are unverified; tests mock AI.

## Open-gap implementation workstream 3 addendum — quote-confirmed Twilio number purchase (2026-10-15)

- Checked current official Twilio [Phone Numbers Pricing](https://www.twilio.com/docs/phone-numbers/pricing), [Voice Pricing](https://www.twilio.com/docs/voice/pricing), and [IncomingPhoneNumber resource](https://www.twilio.com/docs/phone-numbers/api/incomingphonenumber-resource). Monthly price is country/number-type rate-card data, Voice pricing is returned in snake_case, and the IncomingPhoneNumber schema does not document a `price` field. Import now uses the supported pricing endpoints rather than assuming an undocumented property.
- Added bounded Twilio rate lookup, an exact available-number/country check and a three-minute owner/admin quote. The quote shows current recurring monthly and inbound-per-minute prices, excludes taxes/fees, and does not reserve inventory or guarantee a locked price. A conditional D1 quote insertion reserves monthly budget against concurrent quotes and calls. Air-gapped/egress-denied Spaces are rejected before credentials/provider access.
- Added explicit purchase confirmation requiring the quoted monthly amount, correct Space/account/actor, and customer agent binding. Immediately before purchase, the service rechecks number availability and both published rates; it validates the acquired E.164, account, country, and price afterward. Price/persistence failures attempt provider release. Ambiguous provider responses retain a `processing` quote and budget hold; the owner can reconcile by importing the purchased number or explicitly attesting after checking the account that none was created. ## Open-gap workstream 2 refresh — scanned-PDF OCR is implemented locally (2026-10-17)

- Re-audited the OCR path instead of relying on the older plan status. Task-owned PDF bytes are checked against the exact tenant, Space, task, opaque attachment key, and expiration before R2 access. Owner opt-in and Space egress are checked before any hosted OCR call; private/air-gapped Spaces fail closed.
- Scanned PDFs use the separate Document Container with a fixed `pdfinfo`/`pdftoppm` invocation, no network, 1 MiB input, at most five pages, 2048-pixel edge, 5 MiB aggregate PNG output, page-pixel checks, and bounded CPU/time. The host reserves a rolling daily OCR quota for each rendered page, then sends bounded PNGs to the existing Workers AI OCR model. Extracted text is returned as untrusted content; the original PDF and OCR result are not persisted by this route.
- Fresh verification: `email-attachment-route.test.ts` passed (1 file / 9 tests); Agents TypeScript check passed. The test output includes a non-failing pdf.js standard-font warning. These are mocked/local checks; no live Cloudflare Container or Workers AI quality result is claimed.

This is a confirmed architecture mismatch. Preserve its public-ingress security, consent, least privilege, spend, and concurrency protections as general execution/channel policy while moving vertical customer behavior to installed templates/extensions. Number provisioning and generic channel adapters may remain platform infrastructure. Do not add more `customer_bot`-specific behavior or bind public voice calls to the owner planner.
- This is a correction to the earlier Task20 progress statement that proposed joining customer voice into a `customer_bot` project. Task31–32 email/channel requests are later but do not override Task20's explicit customer-extension boundary.
- Initial source scan found the project-kind coupling in `workers/spaces/src/do/SpaceDO.ts`, `workers/spaces/src/routes/recipes.ts`, `workers/agents/src/launchpad/project-templates.ts`, `workers/agents/src/do/NaelaoAgentDO.ts`, `workers/agents/src/index.ts`, `packages/events/src/business-feature-kinds.ts`, and Shell project panels. This list is a starting inventory, not the completed audit.
- Next: finish the full chronological consistency pass over both `Tokenizers vs. Embedding Models Explained` transcript files, then trace each confirmed mismatch to its code and migration boundary. Keep transcript files unchanged. Implement the project-kind/extension migration in small compatibility-preserving steps and record each independently verified/pushed checkpoint.

## Combined transcript consistency audit — baseline findings (2026-10-17)

- Read the visible user turns from both tokenizer transcripts in chronological order and reconciled them against the existing pair-by-pair 33-task continuation ledger and the source-derived main transcript plan. Precedence rule used: a later explicit user correction overrides the conflicting earlier request; model replies are proposals unless the user adopts them; provider, price, latency, and safety claims require independent evidence. The source transcripts remain unchanged.
- **Confirmed source mismatch — external bots:** the early doctor/patient request describes a workflow/extension installed in the patient's own Space and distinguishes knowledge sharing from sharing a live worker. Task19 asks for a built-in owner-to-planner call; Task20 then explicitly says external customer bots remain custom voice/text extensions. Current `customer_bot` project kind, template discriminator, and built-in channel context conflict with this. Correction is recorded in Task20 and the main architecture plan. Security and budget controls must survive as generic policy during migration.
- **Foundry authoring — partial:** current Foundry can edit, test, submit, review, and install structured worker templates, and accepts bounded instructions/source labels/tool allowlists. It does not provide the full artifact-led, nontechnical authoring and representative simulation flow. The transcript plan treats the visual calibration workflow as a product goal, not an adopted exact implementation or certification promise.
- **Installed domain workers and knowledge sharing — partial:** cross-Space template installation and revocable resource grants exist; an explicit accepted-document read path is separate from installing worker instructions. Verify the full grant/accept/revoke path against the doctor/patient boundary before calling it complete. Never copy source data or share a live publisher worker implicitly.
- **FDE requests and Markdown CLI — locally implemented:** owner-reviewed feature-request intake exists; `markdown` is in the virtual CLI and uses the Knowledge Worker. Tests are local/mocked; arbitrary-site fetch/live zone conversion is not claimed.
- **Scope/lifecycle — partial:** Project, Space Assistant, and Space-extension workspaces are distinct, with runtime installation controls. Filesystem sharing between independent extensions remains opt-in/unsettled. Project-to-subspace graduation has a compatibility-preserving logical lifecycle; it does not move physical state. Broad swarm expansion, mass-market localization, and enterprise pricing remain deferred by the later user instruction.
- **Testing/operations — partial:** focused local tests, restart/replay tests, and local Worker dry-runs exist. Full production scenario/load/soak/monitoring and live account acceptance are not established by those results.
- **Context reduction — research/partial:** current context budgeting truncates by character/token estimates and some tools load only selected sections; it is not evidence of source-faithful semantic pruning. The final user query asks about Jev/Clef for oversized inputs but does not adopt the model reply's compression architecture. Any future implementation must keep source recoverable and retrieval traceable.
- Next implementation step: audit the project/channel call graph and migrations, then remove `customer_bot` as a newly creatable structural project kind while preserving existing records and their conservative channel execution policy. Follow with extension-owned behavior dispatch only after defining and enforcing installation identity, declared tools, and explicit data grants. Do not relabel existing built-in behavior as an extension until it actually dispatches through an authenticated installation.

## Extension product composition follow-up — durable workflow parts (2026-10-17)

- Extended the project bundle contract to include executable `workflow` primitives with stable IDs, one `execute(step, ctx)` entry artifact, declared tool names, and an explicit worker role bound to that primitive. The project installer compiles and stores workflow code in the same immutable release as its Canvas, Gadget, and channel parts; it derives only the capabilities required by the actual primitive set before the SpaceDO installation.
- Agents now exposes a membership-checked inventory of active project extension workflows to the existing planner workflow tools. The inventory contains IDs, version, install ID and declared operation names, not channel contacts or credentials. `workflow.run` resolves a project workflow against that exact installation, verifies the host attestation plus the complete current R2 artifact set, pins its code, and runs it through the existing Durable Workflow host and operation authorization. Planner/worker topology was not changed.
- Found and fixed an identity collision: a reused start key could otherwise collide between named installs or different workflows. Durable run IDs now add installation, extension and workflow ID for vertical extensions while preserving legacy IDs for other workflow classes.
- Matched release verification path ordering byte-for-byte with the installer. Locale collation could reorder punctuation in names such as `place-order.ts` and incorrectly reject an otherwise intact release; the integrity test now exercises hyphenated paths.
- Updated the Trading Mentor example to one composed versioned bundle: a project Canvas, isolated project Gadget schema, external WhatsApp student handler, and member-facing delegated `place_order` worker. The worker requires explicit confirmation and bounded order parameters. The example explicitly states the broker operation is deployment-provided, that its schema is not yet written by the sample, and that provider OAuth/session routing and other lifecycle features remain open.
- Current ABI supports a single workflow entry per primitive. Sequential actions belong inside its durable `execute` function; non-empty `steps` paths are rejected rather than stored as unused files. Additional worker parts are separate workflow primitives and role bindings.
- Verification: Agent SDK typecheck and contract/taxonomy suites passed (22 tests); Agents typecheck and workflow host/orchestration/compiler suites passed (10 tests); Spaces and Shell typechecks passed; `git diff --check` passed. No provider or live Cloudflare acceptance was run.
- Remaining extension product gaps include provider-specific OAuth and credential lifecycle, external identity/session routing across multiple bot installations, user/install-scoped channel identities, end-to-end workflow composition UX, Gadget writes from extension UI/workers, publisher identity signatures, release upgrade/rollback UX, and inbox reply composition/assignment. The current host HMAC plus complete R2 digest checks establish host acceptance and artifact integrity, not publisher identity.

## Extension product composition follow-up — inbox handoff controls (2026-10-17)

Extended it with deterministic `(created_at,event_id)` cursor paging, removed raw provider event/contact identifiers from the projection, and surfaced persistent takeover state.
- Added an owner/admin action that revalidates the active installation's tenant, Space, project and scope before pausing automatic handling for one exact inbound event's conversation. In-flight/delivering events return 409 so an admin cannot falsely assume an uncertain provider send was stopped. The UI points the administrator to reply through their connected provider inbox; it does not invent a reply transport.
