## Extension inbox follow-up — per-conversation deletion

- Added owner/admin deletion by inbox event ID. The host resolves the internal conversation key only after checking the exact project and installation, then deletes that conversation's full local transcript, dispatch ledger, takeover state, and legacy/named extension session state. The API never returns the provider contact or conversation ID.
- Deletion returns 409 while processing or provider delivery is active/uncertain, so a concurrent external action cannot be mistaken for erased state. The UI confirms that this removes Naelao's stored data and does not delete copies held by the provider.
- Verification passed: Agents typecheck; focused inbox/session suites (11 tests); Shell typecheck; and `git diff --check`.
- Inbox reply composition and assignment remain open; this action implements local data deletion only.

## Extension runtime follow-up — workflow-owned Gadget data

- Added an SDK `ExtensionWorkflowContext.gadgets` contract and isolated runtime methods for list/get/insert. A workflow must include each operation in its `toolNames`; that list remains an owner-reviewed package declaration and is still intersected with the exact active install at runtime.
- The Agents host requires an active `vertical_extension` run, project, installation, granted operation, and project-member worker role bound to that workflow. The Spaces bridge then rechecks tenant/Space/project/install scope, active status, `PROJECT_BOUND` scope, and that the Gadget primitive belongs to the same bundle. It derives the named instance's Gadget actor ID server-side and forwards only the fixed list/get/insert MCP verbs. No SQL or Gadget ID becomes a public-channel capability.
- Updated the Trading Mentor sample to write its submitted order to the install-scoped trade journal after the broker operation, and bumped the package to `1.2.0` so the changed artifacts remain compatible with immutable releases.

- Follow-up: expanded the bounded API to include owner-declared record update/delete, with schema-column validation, primary-key predicates, fixed action allowlists, installation revalidation, and workflow idempotency. Gadget schema mutation and arbitrary SQL remain unavailable to extension workers.
- Verification for CRUD follow-up passed: SDK, Spaces, Agents, and Shell typechecks; SDK bundle suite (15 tests); Agents workflow compiler/scope suites (9 tests); Spaces Gadget record/bridge suites (3 tests); and `git diff --check`.
- Remaining limits: Canvas-side Gadget actions still need a scoped host UI contract; workflow code does not receive schema migration or arbitrary SQL.

## Extension inbox follow-up — owner/admin replies

- Added a reply action for an owner/admin after pausing a conversation. Text is UTF-8 bounded to 2 KiB and moderation checked, sent only through the existing host `notify.<provider>` operation, and persisted by event ID. One manual reply attempt is allowed per inbound event; sent replies become part of the stored conversation, while uncertain delivery is retained for human reconciliation without automatic resend.
- The handler rejects replies during automated processing/delivery and scopes each event to the exact project and active installation. Conversation deletion removes the reply ledger/content along with the message transcript and session state.

## Universal Agent OS roadmap implementation — Phase 0 inventory (2026-10-17)

- Re-established `/workspace/Naelao-1` on `Mcbt5s-Backup-2`, HEAD `0ca1e0acaa3caebd4025d1acb42de48b39b205e3`; the tree was clean and matched origin before implementation. Available disk at start was 18 GiB.
- Read `AGENTS.md`, `new plan/PROJECT-BOTS-UNIVERSAL-AGENT-OS-ROADMAP.md`, the existing FDE extension architecture and continuation progress, and the transcript reconciliation. Rechecked project agent templates, project-template catalogue, project page/session routes, assistant approval resolution, project spend reservations, extension channel isolation, and template-mode UI.
- Inventory confirmed: the same planner/worker runtime handles Space-wide runs and project-bound runs; project sessions/threads and project agent configuration already exist; `assistant_policy` and `agent_policy_overrides` persist legacy approval modes; SpaceDO has atomic project spend reservations; public channel extensions have a restricted `channel_only` principal. Do not implement a separate planner for Project Bots or route public customer traffic into internal project threads.
- Current gaps for this roadmap: Space Assistant and project policy paths still resolve approval settings differently; the Shell Discover/catalogue page exposes AVE/Cowork sections, Market Radar, wallet-mode badges, and binary “Guided/Autonomous” copy; project Bot roster/proactive scheduler integration is not yet complete; provider credentials, publisher signatures, external install routing, Canvas/Gadget actions, and FDE economics are tracked as open by the architecture plan.
- Baseline scope: repository packages use `corepack pnpm` (pinned 9.12.0); no full-stack attempt was made. A focused Shell typecheck after Phase 1 UI changes passed. No tests were run for the docs-only inventory.
- Phase 1 UI/compatibility slice is implemented: template cards no longer split AVE/Cowork, the Market Radar widget and wallet-mode badges are absent from the general Space creation surface, onboarding autonomy choices use bounded language, and the Shell uses one presentation adapter for legacy approval settings. Legacy catalog metadata remains readable but is deprecated. The adapter is explicitly not an authorization decision; existing server permission, role, grant, approval, and spend gates remain authoritative and were not weakened.
- Verification: Agent SDK typecheck and build passed; the autonomy presentation suite passed (4 tests); Shell typecheck passed; Agents Worker typecheck passed; `git diff --check` passed. No provider/live environment was used.
- Phase 1 implementation limitation: persisted assistant/project approval fields and their runtime evaluators are not migrated or made versioned yet. The current adapter only gives them consistent, accurate UI semantics. Keep this limitation visible while Phase 2 defines the Project Bot planner contract against the existing role-scoped runtime.
- Exact next action: Phase 2 — trace the project thread creation/send path and default agent selection end to end, then make the existing project-scoped planner explicitly visible as the Project Bot, with internal project sessions/threads and no customer-channel ingress.

## Universal Agent OS roadmap implementation — Phase 2 Project Bot access (2026-10-17)

- Added a primary **Bots** destination and project roster. The roster uses authenticated `listProjects`, `listAgentDefinitions`, and `listThreads`; it shows one internal Project Bot per active project, labels scope, shows the last primary-thread activity, and offers Continue, New thread, and Project settings actions.
- Starting a Bot conversation calls the existing authenticated `createThread` with the selected `project_id`; continuing opens the existing project thread route. The existing `ProjectCanvas` passes the verified project context into `Chat`, which sends `projectId` on `/agent/run` and chooses `project.default_agent_id` (or its existing project-scoped fallback). This reuses the same planner/worker runtime as the Space Assistant. Public channel events are excluded from the Bot roster; they remain in extension inbox surfaces.
- Project detail now labels the configured default profile **Project Bot planner**, the default settings choice is **Project Bot default**, and the selected profile badge identifies its planner role. No new planner architecture or planner/worker topology was introduced.
- Verification: Shell `tsc --noEmit` passed after adding the route; `git diff --check` passed. No browser automation is configured in the Shell package, so direct-link/history/mobile behavior remains to verify in later integration coverage.
- Limitations: Project Bot identity is still derived from the project and existing default agent profile rather than stored as a separate first-class Bot record; routine/heartbeat association and presence beyond thread activity are not implemented yet. External extension connections remain independent.
- Exact next action: Phase 3 — add project-scoped durable routines that wake the internal Project Bot planner, with idempotency, no-overlap/missed-tick rules, spend admission, run history, pause/resume, and the read-only Space Project Steward review flow.

## Universal Agent OS roadmap implementation — Phase 3 routine integration (in progress, 2026-10-17)

- Traced the current routine path end to end. Project planner wakes already use `scheduled_jobs` in PLATFORM_DB, the hourly queue dispatcher, run-history rows, failure backoff/auto-disable, and a project-scoped Crons panel with pause/resume, history, and next-run display. The project Bot card now links directly to that routine panel, including when it must first create a project thread.
- Found that `schedule.wake_me` also inserted a second row into SpaceDO `project_scheduled_jobs`, which has no read or dispatch path. This was a non-atomic double write: either side could fail while leaving a schedule that could not be listed or cancelled. Removed that dead registration from the active wake path; `scheduled_jobs` is now the single registry used by the prebuilt schedule tool. Existing legacy rows are not migrated or deleted.
- Added `?panel=crons` deep-link support to Chat, preserving local tab state while allowing Bot cards to land on project routines. Corrected the side-panel scope documentation: project threads show project jobs; the assistant/Home view filters to assistant scope.
- Verification: Agents Worker typecheck passed; Shell typecheck passed; `tests/unit/file-and-schedule-tools.test.ts` passed (10 tests) using `tests/vitest.config.ts`; `git diff --check` passed.
- Phase 3 remains open. The queue dispatcher is at-least-once and its existing busy-agent check is not a durable per-job lease/idempotency claim; missed-tick/coalescing and quiet-hours policy are not consolidated; routine spend admission is not verified end to end; and the Space Project Steward extension/workspace, read-only project portfolio summaries, review ranking, and confirmation flow are not implemented.
- Exact next action: implement and test a durable idempotent dispatch claim and explicit pause/start fence for project routine jobs, then implement the isolated, read-only Space Project Steward installation and review surface without changing planner topology or enabling automatic archive/shutdown.

### Phase 3 routine dispatcher hardening checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- Added a D1 dispatch-claim ledger keyed by `(job_id, sweep_key)`. The queue payload keeps a stable hourly sweep key so redelivery is idempotent; atomic insert requires the job still be enabled and no active per-job lease. The consumer re-checks the current cron due state before claiming, so a stale message after a successful tick does not run again. The claim is terminally marked completed, failed, or skipped; a crashed invocation can be retried after its 30-minute lease expires. Terminal claims are pruned after seven days.
- This closes the race where a pause between enqueue and consume could still start a job, and it prevents duplicate deliveries from re-running a routine within one sweep. The existing DO active-run guard remains an additional overlap check.
- Verification: Agents Worker typecheck passed; scheduled dispatch claim tests passed (3 tests); prior schedule tool tests (10 tests), Shell typecheck, and `git diff --check` remain green from the preceding checkpoint.
- Remaining Phase 3 work: extend tests to exercise the claim against the D1 SQLite runtime and pause/queue race; define/implement missed-tick coalescing and quiet-hours policy; prove routine execution admission is charged/reserved within the correct Project scope; implement the isolated Space Project Steward extension workspace, read-only project portfolio data, ranking/evidence UI, and explicit review confirmation flow.

### Phase 3 portfolio visibility checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- Project Bot cards now show each project's next enabled routine or the number of paused routines. The authenticated roster loads the existing job list once and filters it by project ID.
- Added an explicitly heuristic portfolio review signal for active projects with no recorded activity in at least 30 days, based on the existing `projects.last_activity_at` field. It links to project settings and explicitly states that it does not pause, archive, or delete anything.
- Verification: Shell typecheck and `git diff --check` passed.
- This is an interim read-only UI signal, not the planned Space Project Steward extension. It has no isolated extension instance/workspace, journal/task/extension-health evidence, scheduled review/ranking, confidence record, or confirmation workflow. Those remain Phase 3 blockers.

### Phase 3 schedule visibility and authorization checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- The Bot roster now asks for schedules only in the caller's visible-project scope. The Spaces worker resolves project visibility through the SpaceDO before returning the unfiltered schedule list; an explicit `project_id` lookup and a run-history lookup require read access to that project. A Space member cannot enumerate another member's private project routine metadata by omitting the filter or guessing a project ID.
- Project viewers may read routine schedule metadata; the existing admin floor still controls create, edit, pause, and delete. Legacy assistant-scope jobs remain filtered separately; the Crons/Routines tab is now hidden in Space Assistant sessions because platform automations are project-anchored and the Space Assistant uses its separate Heartbeats surface.
- Added regression coverage for visible-project filtering and denial of a private project's schedule. The old product-root GET expectation assumed root membership without a `space_members` row; the current `resolveEndUserBusinessRole` contract fails closed without an explicit membership, so the stale test now asserts 403.
- Verification: Spaces Worker, Agents Worker, and Shell typechecks passed; all scheduled jobs route/dispatch tests passed (38 tests); `git diff --check` passed.

### Phase 3 claim lease integration tests (2026-10-17)

- Exercised the actual migration and claim SQL against the repository's SQLite-backed D1 test adapter. Concurrent claims for one job/sweep produce exactly one owner; an active lease blocks the next sweep; a paused job cannot be claimed; and a crashed job can be claimed after the 30-minute lease expires.
- A malformed scheduled queue message with no job ID now exits before policy D1 reads.
- Verification: scheduled dispatch SQLite integration tests passed (4 tests); scheduled-jobs route/dispatch tests passed (38 tests); worker typechecks passed in the prior checkpoint.

### Phase 3 deterministic portfolio review API (2026-10-17)

- Added a read-only, membership-checked portfolio endpoint. It resolves visible projects through the SpaceDO, then includes only each visible project's own routine failure counts from PLATFORM_DB. The deterministic ranker prioritizes repeated routine failures (high confidence) over inactivity prompts (low confidence) and returns the exact evidence fields used for each signal.
- The Bots roster now consumes those signals and links to the relevant project. No model output or lifecycle mutation is involved; the API response explicitly marks itself advisory-only.
- Verification: Spaces Worker and Shell typechecks passed; review ranker tests passed (3); API scope test passed (1); scheduled-jobs route/dispatch tests passed (38).
- Phase 3 still lacks the actual platform-maintained Space-wide extension installation and isolated workspace, periodic review execution, journal/task/extension-health evidence, quiet-hours/missed-tick policy, and an attributed confirmation workflow. The current review endpoint is a useful read-only substrate, not completion of that extension requirement.

### Phase 3 routine quiet-hours checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- Added optional local-time quiet windows with IANA timezone validation to routine creation/editing. The scheduler checks the window both before enqueue and again at queue consumption. A due job suppressed during quiet hours remains due and runs once after the window; sweep claims coalesce missed ticks. Routine UI shows the configured window and suppresses the overdue warning while quiet time is active.
- Fixed the scheduled-job business-membership test fixture to include the existing `space_members` migration required by the production fail-closed membership lookup; the authorization behavior did not change.
- Verification: Types, Spaces Worker, Agents Worker, and Shell typechecks passed; scheduled-jobs route/dispatch/authorization and timezone tests passed (58 tests); `git diff --check` passed.
- Phase 3 remains incomplete: the platform-managed Space Project Steward instance, its isolated extension workspace, periodic execution, richer journal/task/extension-health evidence, and attributed reversible retirement review flow are still open.

### Phase 3 steward summary grant checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- Added `space.project_steward.read_summary.v1`, an internal-HMAC-only endpoint for a future platform-managed steward extension. It returns active project IDs/names, creation/activity timestamps, and failed routine counts. It exposes no project content, files, members, planner tools, or lifecycle mutation operation.
- Verification: Spaces Worker typecheck passed; steward ranker and route tests passed (5 tests), including unauthenticated denial and archived-project exclusion; `git diff --check` passed.
- This is the read-only grant substrate only. The platform-managed extension instance/workspace, timer that invokes it, richer project journal/task/extension health evidence, advisory ranking from its run, and user-attributed reversible retirement workflow remain Phase 3 exit criteria.

### Phase 3 scheduled steward report checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- Added a daily queue roster for every active Space and a D1-backed per-Space/day claim lease. Queue retries cannot generate duplicate reports; failed runs release their lease for retry, while successful runs persist a deterministic advisory ranking for the UI.
- The run reads only `space.project_steward.read_summary.v1`, uses the shared deterministic ranker, and stores an optional JSON archive under the Space-wide steward workspace namespace. The Spaces UI prefers the latest saved report but re-filters it against the caller's current visible project IDs, so stored results do not grant access after membership changes.
- Verification: Agents and Spaces typechecks passed; steward dispatch/review and scheduled-jobs tests passed (48 tests); `git diff --check` passed.
- At this checkpoint the scheduled service had not yet provisioned a platform extension record; the next checkpoint below closes that identity/lifecycle gap. Richer journal/task/extension-health evidence and user-attributed reversible retirement recommendations and confirmation flow remain open. The report remains advisory and performs no project or extension lifecycle action.

### Phase 3 platform Steward installation checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- Added the named `ext_project_steward` platform manifest and an internal-HMAC-only, idempotent provisioning endpoint. Provisioning goes through SpaceDO's normal `/extensions` installation lifecycle as `SPACE_SHARED`, returning a stable installation ID. The manifest declares no code, gadgets, channels, daemons, commands, or broad permissions; it names only the Steward summary capability.
- The scheduled report archive now uses that returned installation ID for the dedicated Space extension workspace path. Installation setup and report reads are covered by route tests; the existing SpaceDO lifecycle owns install identity and persistence.
- Verification: Agents and Spaces typechecks passed; Steward install/report and route tests passed (8 tests); `git diff --check` passed.
- Limitation: scheduled collection/ranking still runs as deterministic platform host code; this installation has no user-authored executable bundle and no LLM explanation. The typed summary API remains its data grant. Richer evidence and an attributed, reversible review/confirmation workflow are still open Phase 3 work.

### Phase 3 project routine thread attribution checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- Goal-based scheduled runs with a `project_id` now create a structural thread in that project, create a heartbeat session linked to that thread, and dispatch the same planner/worker path with the verified project ID. A missing project thread/session fails closed instead of silently falling back to a Space Assistant session. Space-wide scheduled work keeps its existing Space Assistant heartbeat session behavior.
- Verification: scheduled-jobs dispatch tests passed (42 tests), including assertions for project thread/session linking and exact project scope; Agents typecheck and `git diff --check` passed.
- Direct script routines continue using their existing isolated direct execution path. Durable run-to-thread replay reuse and richer routine result delivery remain open; the dispatch claim prevents normal queue duplicate creation for the same routine sweep.

### Phase 3 portfolio evidence checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Expanded the internal Steward summary with compact per-project pending, in-progress, and blocked task counts and active extension-install counts. The SpaceDO query returns no task titles, files, member identities, or conversation content.
- The deterministic ranker now prioritizes blocked work with high confidence, retains routine failure priority, and attaches pending-work/active-extension evidence to stale-project check-ins. The Shell explains the evidence and links to the project; all recommendations remain advisory.
- Verification: Agents, Spaces, and Shell typechecks passed; project-steward review/report and SpaceDO fact suites passed (25 tests); `git diff --check` passed.
- Still open in Phase 3: journal summaries, extension run/connection health and spend evidence, typed decision records with actor attribution, and the reversible owner-confirmed retirement review flow. No project lifecycle action was added.

### Phase 3 project lifecycle scheduler fence (2026-10-18)

- Added a typed SpaceDO lifecycle lookup and an HMAC-only Spaces Worker proxy. Before claiming or invoking a queued project routine, Agents rechecks the authoritative project state. Archived, hibernated, missing, and otherwise inactive projects are acknowledged without planner/script execution; an unavailable lifecycle check retries the queue and fails closed. Space-wide jobs are unchanged.
- Verification: Agents and Spaces typechecks passed; scheduled-job, project lifecycle, and Steward route tests passed (61 tests); `git diff --check` passed.
- The check and subsequent invocation are separate service calls, so this does not provide a transactionally atomic fence against a project being archived in the few milliseconds after the check. Lifecycle archive/restore still needs a coordinated routine pause/restore record and full impact confirmation flow.

### Phase 4 external usage controls checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- Added project setting `external_daily_message_cap` (0 = unlimited, maximum 10,000) and exposed it in Project settings as a per-person daily limit for external channel traffic.
- External sender identity is keyed with HMAC-SHA256 over tenant, Space, Project, installation, provider, and sender ID. Only the opaque digest is stored; raw provider IDs are not copied into quota records.
- The allowance is atomically counted in the shared SpaceDO, partitioned by project, installation, provider, digest, and UTC day. This keeps the cap effective across a customer's multiple conversations/AgentDOs. The worker endpoint is HMAC-gated and checks the project is in the addressed Space. A reached cap or unavailable quota check prevents extension execution and switches the conversation to human review.
- Verification: Agents, Spaces, and Shell typechecks passed; project config/quota suites passed (15 tests); `git diff --check` passed. A broader older project-config fail-closed suite was also run and had five fixture/behavior failures unrelated to this quota slice; logged as candidates for the separate Phase 7 test track.
- Phase 4 remains open: actual per-installation provider OAuth/credential lifecycle, customer identity routing across named instances, provider webhook lifecycle/revocation, robust spend/credit ceilings, abuse response and human settings still need implementation and end-to-end coverage.

### Phase 4 quota decision replay checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- Added a per-message quota decision ledger in SpaceDO. Each external message event is allowed or denied once; retries with the same event ID return the original result and do not increment usage a second time. Sender digests, project/install/provider/day, cap and resulting count are retained for 30 days, then pruned.
- Verification: Agents and Spaces typechecks passed; quota and real SpaceDO integration suites passed (15 tests), including event replay; `git diff --check` passed.

### Phase 4 quota exhaustion alert checkpoint (2026-10-17)

- A sender that keeps messaging after reaching the daily cap still enters human takeover, but generates at most one owner notification per sender/extension/provider/day. Each over-limit event retains its own replay-safe allow/deny decision. This prevents a single abusive sender from flooding the owner inbox while preserving the audit and handoff state.
- Verification: Agents and Spaces typechecks passed; external quota and SpaceDO integration suites passed (15 tests); `git diff --check` passed.

### Phase 4 cross-install quota checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Corrected project-level external daily caps to aggregate the same sender/provider across every extension installation in the project. Opaque sender hashes now omit installation identity for the shared counter, while decision records retain the installation ID for audit. Sender identities remain HMAC-derived; no raw provider IDs are stored.
- Existing per-install counters are lazily folded into the shared daily counter on that installation's next message and removed atomically. This carries forward that installation's current-day usage during rollout. Per-event replay keys are now scoped by installation/provider/event ID so unrelated provider accounts with a colliding event ID cannot replay each other's allow decision.
- Owner exhaustion alerts deduplicate at the project/sender/provider/day level. All denied events still have their own decision and enter human takeover.
- Verification: Agents and Spaces typechecks passed; quota and SpaceDO lifecycle tests passed (16 tests); `git diff --check` passed.
- Remaining Phase 4 gaps: cost-rate versioning and unified usage events for inference, execution, storage, and connectors; install/run budgets beyond the shared external message cap; provider-level spend controls; owner off-topic thresholds and alert settings; payer/credit UI and failure reconciliation across all charged paths.
- Updated the Project settings copy to say the cap is shared across every extension instance in that project and provider, matching the enforced scope.

### Phase 4 direct external-answer billing checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- The customer-extension path that asks the host to answer from owner-published Knowledge now records actual model usage, settles its project daily-cap reservation, and debits the project's wallet with an idempotent message-scoped key. When a provider omits token usage, the pre-inference estimate is used for accounting.
- If inference fails after reservation, the reservation is settled at zero to release the hold immediately instead of waiting for its lease to expire. Delivery failures after a completed inference do not erase its cost.
- Verification: focused restricted-answer and settlement tests passed (11 tests); Agents Worker typecheck and `git diff --check` passed. An initial typecheck caught an exact-optional-property typing issue in the optional usage evidence; corrected before the passing run.
- Phase 4 remains open for unified, versioned usage events across inference/execution/storage/connectors, broader install/run and provider spend budgets, abuse policy settings, and usage reconciliation/UI.

### Phase 4 versioned usage ledger checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Added migration 0109 for usage-event schema v2: unit, rate-card version, cost credits, project/install/run IDs, model/provider, connector provider, runtime tier, opaque subject digest, payer kind, and decision. Legacy records migrate as schema v1 and remain included in existing plan-quota aggregates.
- Billing ingestion now validates v2 units, dimensions, payer/decision enums, idempotency, bounded values, and 64-hex subject digests. It records full dimensions in D1 and forwards them to the metering queue only for plan/platform/unknown payers; wallet-prepaid records are not charged again through the platform subscription rail. Plan quota aggregates explicitly exclude v2 events paid by project wallets.
- Added `core-model-rates-v1`; OpenRouter usage records the exact observed catalog timestamp as its rate-card version. Restricted external answers now emit project/install/run scoped token and cost events, and runtime execution emits versioned infra-credit events. Existing project cap and wallet ledgers remain the admission/settlement authorities.
- Verification: Billing Worker, Agents Worker, and billing-core typechecks passed; billing route, real migration/runtime insertion, quota reservation, restricted-answer and billing settlement tests passed (34 tests); `git diff --check` passed.
- Phase 4 remains open: other planner/user inference paths, storage bytes, connector calls, workflow execution, and provider billing events are not yet all emitted through v2; installation/run/provider-specific ceilings, off-topic budgets/alerts, owner payer visibility, and failed charge/usage reconciliation are still required.

### Phase 4 project planner usage checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Normal project planner runs now append one idempotent v2 `project_llm_tokens` event per run, with project/space/run IDs, model/provider, actual token quantity, priced credits, rate-card version and payer. Existing cap and wallet settlement remains authoritative; prepaid wallet usage is not sent to the platform subscription meter.
- Added focused event-construction tests. The adjacent infrastructure metering suite exposed stale expectations about the current resource table and helper return values; updated those assertions to the established contract (negative quantities reject, container allocation is charged by the canonical resource unit, and reporting returns its documented credit result).
- Verification: Agents Worker typecheck passed; 4 focused Agents tests and 27 focused ledger/migration/metering tests passed; `git diff --check` passed. The broader Agents and unit suites have not been rerun.
- Phase 4 remains open for inference-producing extension paths outside the main planner and restricted-answer flows, storage bytes, connector calls, workflow execution, provider billing, broader install/run/provider-specific ceilings, off-topic budgets/alerts, owner payer visibility and failed charge/usage reconciliation.

### Phase 4 non-project planner payer accounting checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Space Assistant builder inference reservation settlement now writes its existing `llm_tokens` row as schema v2 with model/provider, cost, rate-card version and explicit `plan_quota` payer. It continues to settle the reservation atomically and emit the plan meter only once; the non-reserved fallback now uses the same versioned contract.
- Product end-user assistant inference now records a v2 `consumer_credits` event after its existing user credit settlement. This retains usage and model cost in the ledger without entering the organization plan quota or subscription meter.
- Added real-SQLite settlement-route coverage proving a builder hold becomes one dimensioned v2 event and one platform meter event.
- Verification: Agents and Billing Worker typechecks passed; focused route, helper and existing usage tests passed (33 tests); `git diff --check` passed. Broader Agents suite not rerun.

### Phase 4 workflow execution usage checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Workflow step reporting now emits v2 `infra_credits` with cost, rate card, plan-quota payer, stable run/idempotency identity, and optional project/install/runtime-tier scope.
- Added migration 0110 to persist project, installation, and runtime tier on workflow budget reservations. Workflow and container callers pass these dimensions at reservation time; mismatched dimensions on a reservation replay fail with `reservation_scope_conflict`. Settlement writes one plan-quota v2 infra event and forwards the same scoped dimensions once to the metering queue.

### Phase 4 customer connector-attempt ledger checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Public customer-channel reply delivery now writes an idempotent v2 `connector_calls` event for each attempted provider delivery, including provider, project, installation, run, and an opaque subject digest. Failed or uncertain sends are retained as failed attempts; they are not automatically retried or counted as successful delivery.
- No connector price is invented. These events use `payer_kind=unknown` and no cost, so they support audit and later reconciliation while the current Billing queue remains log-only. Provider-specific charges and pricing evidence remain open.
- Verification: Agents and Billing Worker typechecks passed; focused Agents usage/external-answer/workflow tests passed (9 tests) and real-SQLite usage/workflow ledger tests passed (4 tests); `git diff --check` passed.

### Phase 4 Knowledge storage-byte ledger checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Knowledge ingestion now totals UTF-8/original PDF bytes actually accepted by its R2 puts: original source, serialized chunks, citation chunks, and the optional AutoRAG mirror. It records the aggregate under the Space/Project/document idempotency key after successful ingestion, or records the partial successful writes if later ingestion fails.
- The Knowledge Worker now has a Billing Worker service binding. Its event is `storage_bytes_written` with unit `bytes`, project scope, no invented `cost_credits`, and unknown payer until a real storage pricing/payer contract exists. This measures bytes written, not retained GB-months or a full bucket inventory.
- Verification: Knowledge typecheck and Wrangler dry-run passed with the new service binding; storage-report helper tests (2), real-SQLite usage-event tests (4), and `git diff --check` passed.
- Remaining: storage accounting for extension artifacts, conversation sessions, attachments and archives; overwrite/delete reconciliation, retained-byte snapshots, pricing attribution, and workspace/install storage ceilings.

### Phase 4 usage delivery reconciliation checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- `NaelaoAgentDO` now persists canonical billing events to a local SQLite outbox before sending. Failed/non-2xx delivery records an error and uses bounded exponential retry; the DO alarm coordinates outbox retries with active-run watchdog deadlines. Successful but ambiguously acknowledged requests are safe to replay through the stable event idempotency key.
- The outbox is tenant-scoped, rejects missing idempotency keys and oversized payloads, and removes delivered entries after 30 days. Billing event hashes now include the verified tenant, so equal idempotency strings from different tenants cannot suppress each other.
- Verification: Agents and Billing Worker typechecks passed; outbox retry/backoff and builder helper tests passed (6 tests); real-SQLite usage, project budget, and reservation ledger tests passed (7 tests); `git diff --check` passed.
- This reconciles event transport, not all payer outcomes: wallet debit failures are still surfaced as run notes, but durable reconciliation between provider charge, project/user wallet, plan queue, and event ledger remains open.

### Phase 3 direct scheduled-script budget checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Direct event/scheduled scripts with a Project scope now re-read the SpaceDO project policy, enforce the project's execution-tier ceiling, check the shared Space wallet before running, and reserve one credit against a configured daily Project cap. This covers the bounded single isolate/container infrastructure charge; Tier 1/no-cost executions settle the hold at zero.
- After execution, the host records infrastructure usage and settles the Project cap plus wallet using a run-scoped idempotency key. A thrown execution releases the held amount. Projectless Space-wide scripts preserve their existing behavior.
- Verification: Agents Worker typecheck passed; scheduled dispatch and adjacent restricted-answer suites passed (14 tests); `git diff --check` passed.
- This closes direct-script policy admission, not full routine economics: project goal runs reserve model inference through the normal planner entrypoint, while a unified per-run reservation covering both inference and every possible execution/storage/connector resource remains a Phase 4 requirement. Dispatch-to-DO at-most-once replay for ambiguous direct-script execution also remains open.

### Phase 3 attributed Steward review checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Added evidence-keyed owner/admin review decisions for portfolio signals. A changed recommendation or evidence set gets a new key and cannot inherit a prior dismissal. Acknowledgement records actor and optional note; snooze hides that exact signal for seven days; dismissal hides it until its evidence changes.
- Members may read current visible-project signals, but only an owner/admin can record decisions. The endpoint verifies current project visibility and accepts only the non-mutating actions; it has no archive, pause, delete, or shutdown operation. The Bots page exposes these controls only to owner/admin users.
- Verification: Spaces and Shell typechecks passed; project-steward route/ranker/key tests passed (11 tests); `git diff --check` passed. Tests include member denial, actor attribution, acknowledgment display, and dismissal filtering.
- The complete reversible retirement workflow is still open because project archive/restore lacks a coordinated routine fence, impact preview, restore ledger, and explicit owner confirmation. Steward recommendations remain advisory.

### Phase 4 project usage owner view checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Added an HMAC-authenticated Billing Worker project-summary endpoint that returns measured credits, event totals, unpriced-event totals, and a breakdown grouped by event type, unit, payer, extension installation, inference provider, and connector provider. Headline totals are computed independently of the bounded 200-group breakdown, so truncation cannot understate totals.
- Added a Spaces API proxy that checks owner/admin membership and verifies the caller can manage the exact project before requesting the tenant-bound Billing summary. The project page now shows a selectable 1/7/30/90 day view, payer labels, measured units and source identifiers, and explicitly labels events with unknown price. Scope changes clear prior data while loading; authorization and API failures do not expose summary data.

It does not settle missing provider prices or cover custom extension inference/storage paths that have not yet emitted usage records. Unified run-level caps, provider-native spend enforcement, owner-configurable off-topic/abuse budgets, and payer outcome reconciliation remain Phase 4 work.

### Phase 5 project bundle runtime revocation checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Added a SpaceDO lookup that maps project Canvas/Gadget actor IDs back to current and historical bundle declarations for the exact tenant, Space, and project. It verifies the active manifest against its recorded host HMAC release attestation and distinguishes an unmanaged project app from a removed or obsolete extension resource.
- Project Canvas/Gadget routes now fail closed when a package is removed, its Canvas/Gadget primitive disappears in an upgrade, or the current release attestation is invalid. Package-managed actors cannot be reinitialized through the generic project Gadget route; the reviewed bundle installer remains the update path. Existing generic project apps remain supported.
- Verification: Spaces typecheck passed; project Gadget route and extension install/runtime suites passed (17 tests), including named actor mapping, upgrade removal of an old Canvas ID, uninstall revocation, invalid attestation denial, and unmanaged legacy project apps; `git diff --check` passed.
- This prevents a retained Canvas/Gadget actor from remaining publicly project-reachable after package removal or an invalid upgrade. It does not delete retained Gadget data. Asymmetric publisher identity, full release artifact validation on Canvas load, version rollback that restores all runtime components/state, and install health/migration recovery remain open Phase 5 work.
- Follow-up Canvas integrity: package installation now passes a host-signed binding from the exact extension ID/version/manifest/release digest/path to the Canvas HTML digest. `MicroAppDO` verifies the release HMAC and Canvas binding at initialization and every render; a package-managed Canvas cannot be overwritten through a generic initialize request, and tampered HTML or proof fails closed. This proves the exact Canvas member was accepted alongside that immutable package digest; it does not rescan every other R2 package artifact during Canvas render and does not establish an FDE publisher identity.
- Verification: Spaces typecheck passed; `public-dynamic-pages`, project Gadget route, and extension install/runtime suites passed (20 tests); `git diff --check` passed. No deployed Worker or live R2 check was performed.

### Phase 5 trusted publisher signature checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Added a per-tenant/Space trusted publisher-key registry with P-256 public keys, stable SHA-256 key IDs, terminal revocation, owner/admin registration controls, and a canonical SDK signing helper over the normalized manifest plus the exact original artifact path/source map. The composed project-bundle installer verifies the trusted key and signature before storing artifacts or installing the package; the UI exposes key registration/revocation and signature entry without sending private key material.
- The accepted publisher ID, key ID, signature, payload digest, and release digest are immutable release metadata. Runtime checks now consult the active publisher-key record for workflow installation, Canvas/Gadget requests, and internal external-channel routing in addition to SpaceDO revocation state. A revoked historical version cannot be rolled back into service. Host acceptance HMAC and publisher identity remain separate claims.
- Verification: SDK, Spaces, and Shell typechecks passed; SDK signature and D1 migration tests passed (8 tests); SpaceDO release/runtime, Gadget runtime, and channel install suites passed (27 tests); `git diff --check` passed.
- Phase 5 remains open: full release artifact-set verification on every runtime load, package migrations/health and state-safe rollback, signed-release coverage for legacy install entrypoints, and install recovery/retention behavior still need completion. No deployed migration or live Worker test was performed.

The Shell inbox supports claiming and releasing an event for the current owner/admin.

### Phase 6 Slack installation connection checkpoint (2026-10-18)

- Added migration 0112 for tenant/Space/project/install/connection-scoped provider credentials and account routing. Slack OAuth now starts only for a declared connection on an active, publisher-verified project extension; the short-lived callback ticket is single-use and the bot token is written only to the credentials vault. The stored connection records workspace identity, granted scopes, and the exact channel-handler digest.
- Signed Slack Events API requests route by workspace ID to that exact installation and connection. Before accepting an event, Agents rechecks the active signed release and current handler bytes; a stale/revoked connection cannot fall through to a legacy Space channel binding. Attachments and replies use that installation's credential. Slack sender session keys are isolated by installation and provider connection, and channel/thread reply destinations are stored separately from sender identity.

Explicit disconnect and package uninstall revoke the provider token best-effort, mark the local record revoked, and delete its vaulted credential. Notifications refuses to fall back to shared credentials when an installation-scoped send was requested.

Slack workspaces are intentionally single-routed within a Space until explicit per-conversation routing exists. ### Phase 6 WhatsApp per-install setup and inbound routing checkpoint (2026-10-18)

## Universal AI OS roadmap implementation checkpoint — 2026-10-20

- The latest user asked to implement the full universal AI OS roadmap serially, adding newly found code gaps to the plan and continuing through the gates. Started from Phase 0/Phase 1 against current `work` branch; repository is not yet release complete.
- Fresh code audit found a high-impact delayed-approval flaw: the loop previously let a resumed action through by `action_name` only, although the model re-evaluates its decision after resume. The public `/script/run` JSON also carried a `preApprovedActions` property, so approval evidence was not structurally limited to the internal resume path.
- Implemented exact approval evidence: canonical argument JSON, action identity/run binding, and an effective approval-policy fingerprint are saved with the suspension checkpoint; resume verifies the authoritative HITL record and only then supplies evidence through an internal method parameter. A changed action input or policy opens a new approval; mismatched/legacy approval rows fail closed. Client-supplied bypass fields are ignored. Human-answer resumes retain their answer path.
- Added regressions for changed arguments and changed policy; exact unchanged arguments still proceed. Updated the Phase 1 roadmap gate and `CLAUDE.md` session notes.
- Verification: durable-approval, loop multi-step, approval-consume, and pending-run suites passed 38/38; Agents TypeScript check passed; `git diff --check` passed. The loop suite had five pre-existing failures because its fake R2 bucket did not implement `head`, ETags, metadata, or conditional writes required by the current CAS contract; repaired the fixture without weakening behavior assertions and updated its write calls to include `expectedHash`. No deployment or live approval journey has been exercised.
- The exact-approval slice was committed as `f4c82ea7` and confirmed at `origin/work`.
- Follow-up audit found that ordinary `/script/run` JSON could supply `humanAnswer`, `resumeSteps`, `ledgerKey`, and `deliberationRound`. Moved these values to a private continuation argument populated only from the Durable Object's persisted interrupted/approval checkpoint. Approval evidence and answered-question text now enter the loop only through that same server-side call path. Interrupted-run continuation now calls the handler with its state directly; it no longer serializes replay state through the route body.
- Added an edge-route regression that sends all four forged continuation fields to the authenticated public run route and asserts none reaches the Durable Object.
- That test exposed a route-order bug: the earlier `/agents/*` Cloudflare Agent handler converted `null` into a 404 and shadowed all later normal agent HTTP endpoints, including `/agents/:agentId/script/run`. Changed it to fall through to Hono when the Agent RPC router has no match. The authenticated run route and its rejection cases now execute instead of returning 404.
- Verification: after fixing the route shadow, the script-run auth/forwarding suite passed 8/8 including the forged continuation-state regression; Agents typecheck and the four approval/resume suites passed 38/38; `git diff --check` passed. No deployment or live approval journey has been exercised.
- Next Phase 1 audit found autonomy policy was read at run admission only. Added a host callback in the script loop that refreshes the effective policy before every action gate and updates the policy fingerprint used for approval-resume evidence. Space Assistant policy is re-read from SpaceDO without the 30-second ingress cache; project-agent `requiresApprovalFor` is re-read from its R2 config. A refresh error switches to ask-first for every action. `hitlGate` now denies gated actions if the HITL service is absent, rather than treating missing infrastructure as approval. Added regressions for mid-run revocation, refresh failure and missing HITL service.

The same suite's operation-hint completeness test found eight project-coordination operations undocumented; added bounded parameter guidance, including owner-only merge constraints and read-only diff/status semantics.
- Added a regression proving a failed per-action policy refresh cannot reuse a permissive run-entry snapshot; the action is parked for approval.
- Extended approval fingerprints with authoritative versions: SpaceDO policy `updated_at` and the project agent-config R2 ETags. This invalidates a pending approval if policy values change and later return to the same values during the suspension.
- Made the revision sources monotonic: SpaceDO policy updates advance `updated_at` by at least one millisecond even if writes share a clock tick; R2 CAS writes do the same for per-object `updatedAt` metadata, even when restored content yields the same content hash. Added a round-trip regression for same-content R2 restoration.
- Found that the project Settings autonomy panel persisted `agent_policy_overrides` but project runs ignored it and consulted only R2 `requiresApprovalFor`. Project runs now resolve the authoritative SpaceDO agent policy at admission and before each action. Profile `requiresApprovalFor` actions remain forced approvals even under an autonomy allowlist; the forced set is included in each approval fingerprint. Added forced-gate regression coverage.

### ScaleDown native compression host admission (2026-10-18)

Native compression is now callable through the prebuilt operation when both are configured; it remains unavailable when the credential or explicit account rate is missing/invalid. The OpenRouter key is never sent to the native API.
- Native compression passes the Space's exact `scaledown/compress` model-policy check, existing project/product-user/builder budget reservation and specialist usage settlement. Reservation estimates now accept explicit host pricing for providers outside the general model catalog. Configured prices are bounded to positive finite values at no more than $1,000/M input tokens; output pricing is zero because the native usage response has no output token count. No unsupported vendor price was assumed.

Biome on the two large pre-existing Agents files reports numerous unrelated historical diagnostics, so it was not used as a clean whole-file gate.

### Durable document partition, workflow and derivative cache (2026-10-18)

- Added a host `document.partition` operation for active project/Space workspace files. It checks private/local placement and delegated path grants before reading bytes; caps source bytes at 10 MiB; extracts UTF-8 text or safe text from unencrypted PDFs up to 100 pages and 1.5 MiB; retains page and UTF-16 offsets; and returns exact source bytes' SHA-256. Scanned PDFs still require the separate owner-enabled OCR path.
- Added a source generator to the Agent SDK for an extension workflow that partitions once, summarizes chunks with deterministic effect keys, merges the result, and returns source/hash/page/offset citations. These calls go through the existing signed workflow identity, pinned artifact, per-step durable effect receipts, membership and project grants, policy/key/catalog gates, and normal run-budget settlement. Added a per-workflow 48 document-operation ceiling.
- Added opt-in derivative caching through the existing `AGENT_CACHE` KV binding with seven-day expiration. Keys include tenant, Space, project/Space scope, actor, current model policy, model, exact source text/ID and task options; the caller reopens or re-retrieves the source before lookup. Only validated derivatives up to 512 KiB are cached. Hits report zero new provider usage and an explicit cache-hit field. The cache is OFF unless `SCALEDOWN_DERIVATIVE_CACHE_ENABLED=true`.
- Verification: Agents typecheck passed; SDK suite passed (106 tests); targeted worker suites passed (35 tests), including a valid two-page PDF fixture, malformed/binary/size-limit checks, Unicode/page chunk provenance, cache actor isolation/zero second provider call, workflow compilation and declaration, and document scope checks; Biome passed on all 10 changed SDK/worker TypeScript files; `git diff --check` passed. No actual paid provider calls were made.
- Current limits: PDFs are text extraction only, with 10 MiB/100-page/1.5 MiB text limits. Scanned PDFs, richer office formats and direct image transport are not supported by this partitioner. The seven-day KV cache is feature-flagged. Workflow tool calls are durable; model-quality comparisons and Shell Compact migration are still open.

### Extreme transcript and bounded API stress audit (2026-10-18)

- Added exact-ID transcript paging to `conversation.search`, with a host-owned current-session scope for both SpaceDO web threads and AgentDO channel threads. Search scans at most 10,000 messages / 20 MiB, preserves Unicode and short technical identifiers, and reports incomplete scans or storage failures without claiming that an unsearched topic was absent. The current thread ID comes from the host context; a model-supplied foreign session ID is rejected.
- Raised the transcript reader from one page to bounded paging (10,000 messages / 40 MiB), while ordinary turns fetch only the newest 500 messages. A full scan happens only when compaction is due; it verifies Space, project, and thread scope on every request and reuses the fetched transcript for compaction. If the full scan exceeds its bound or fails, the planner keeps the recent page and gets an explicit incomplete-history warning instead of losing the whole conversation. Channel transcript reads page by stable rowid and stay inside the host-selected channel session.
- Made durable summary coverage cursor-based with `covered_through_id`, including an existing-DO schema migration. Legacy count-only coverage is rebuilt, removed cursors reset the summary, and summarization checkpoints batches of at most 80 messages / 80,000 characters (up to 20 batches per invocation). Auto-compaction resumes as backlog is added; the model is told that compacted history can be rechecked with `conversation.search`.
- Fixed unbounded request limits found during route and DO query review: SQLite's `LIMIT -1` could expose every memory/space row; several D1/DO list routes lacked a strict upper bound; invalid gadget limits could reach SQL as `NaN`; marketplace offsets had no explicit cap; and concurrency callers could raise a per-key slot limit arbitrarily. Added a shared safe-integer clamp and bounded the reviewed memory recall, scripts, directory, thread, knowledge, admin, search, mini-AGI, SpaceDO activity/notification/signal, gadget, and marketplace query paths. The notification escalation window is capped at 30 days.
- Hardened two previously untested DO boundaries found in the inventory: FeatureFlagDO now validates state, rollout percentage, targeting-rule shape/size, and documented evaluation-context fields; ConformanceSandboxDO now returns client errors for malformed JSON/invalid oracle assertions, does not seal the fixture on a rejected assertion, and rejects duplicate preparation cleanly.
- Added first lifecycle authorization regressions for the two other DOs with no prior test references: BrowserSessionDO rejects malformed scopes and prevents another actor from closing a session; WorkflowRunDO persists initialization and rechecks Space membership on resume. These cover key ownership gates, not the full browser-provider or workflow-provider failure matrix.
- Added stress regressions for 1,234-message search, the exact 10,000-message scan boundary and overflow, 1,205-message channel history, 1,205-message compaction with durable cursor/idempotency, 1,105 messages sharing one timestamp, cross-thread scope rejection, invalid/negative limits, and 100-slot concurrency admission.
- Verification: Agents full suite passed (128 files / 535 tests); Spaces full suite passed (54 files / 183 tests); bounded-integer unit tests passed (3); Threads focused route test passed (1); BrowserSessionDO focused tests passed (2). Direct TypeScript checks passed for Agents, Spaces, Threads, Admin, Knowledge, Search, Mini-AGI, Marketplace, Browser, `packages/http`, and `packages/agent-sdk`. `git diff --check` passed. The earlier first run exposed an incomplete alarm mock in the new concurrency test; the harness was fixed and the final Agents suite passed.
- Scope and remaining coverage: repository inventory identified 32 Workers, 21 Durable Object classes, 13 workflow entrypoints, 8 container runtime files, and 681 route-dispatch references. This checkpoint stresses transcript paths and bounded query inputs, and ran complete Agents and Spaces unit suites plus changed-worker typechecks. Regression coverage now references the four previously uncovered DOs, but the new BrowserSessionDO and WorkflowRunDO tests cover only key ownership gates. This is not an exhaustive failure-injection matrix for every Worker route, every Durable Object alarm/restart/concurrency path, all 13 workflow entrypoints, every container runtime, or deployed D1/R2/KV/provider behavior. No deployed Cloudflare or paid provider calls were made.

### WAF body allocation bound (2026-10-20)

- Closed a security gap found in the audit ledger: WAF JSON was parsed before its recursive limits ran, allowing arbitrarily large input allocation. The WAF now bounds cloned JSON request streams to 1 MiB before parsing and returns 413 when the limit is exceeded. It checks streamed byte count even without a trustworthy Content-Length and leaves the original body available to the endpoint.
- Added regressions for declared oversize, streamed oversize, and safe downstream body readability. The first stream test found that awaiting cancellation on a tee'd clone can block on the unread original branch; cancellation is now initiated without awaiting. Security package typecheck passed; all 20 security test files / 102 tests passed; `git diff --check` passed.
- Refreshed the audit ledger: configured full Vitest workspace is 286 files / 1,258 tests. Biome checks passed for both changed WAF files. The root `pnpm test` wrapper did not complete because Turbo launched package-install subprocesses that failed network fetches with `EAI_AGAIN`; the direct configured workspace command passed. This does not close the older root-config failure triage, live deployment/provider checks, or exhaustive Worker/DO/workflow/container matrix.
- A follow-up WAF failure-path review found that a locked/uncloneable JSON stream could bypass scanning. The WAF now returns `503 waf_inspection_unavailable` and does not call the endpoint when inspection cannot run. Added a regression for a preceding middleware locking the body. The 8 WAF stress tests, all 20 security test files / 103 tests, security typecheck, Biome, and `git diff --check` passed. The full-workspace run above preceded this one added regression; no deployment test was run.

### Chat upload multipart allocation bound (2026-10-20)

- Attachment review found the chat upload endpoint called Hono `parseBody()` before applying its 20 MiB per-file rule, with no aggregate cap. Added a streaming, non-buffering clone check before multipart parsing, a 61 MiB request ceiling (60 MiB file bytes + 1 MiB bounded multipart overhead), and a 60 MiB total file ceiling. Locked/uncloneable input returns 503; oversized requests return 413.
- Exported `checkRequestBodyByteLimit` from `@naelao/security`. Its tests cover declared size, chunked overflow with missing Content-Length, preserving a bounded original stream, and locked stream failure. ### Telegram aggregate media bound (2026-10-20)

- Telegram accepted five individual files up to 5 MiB each but had no 15 MiB aggregate preflight, unlike Slack/Discord/WhatsApp and the shared attachment store. Added a declared-size running-total guard and actual-byte guard for absent size metadata; values below `-1` are rejected rather than treated as unknown.
- Added stress cases with 5 MiB x 3 actual downloads and a fourth declared or unknown-size file; both fail at the fourth metadata lookup before downloading that body. The bounded stream reader also uses only the remaining aggregate allowance when metadata is unknown. Telegram tests passed 4/4, Agents typecheck passed, Biome and `git diff --check` passed. No external Bot API call was made.
- The same audit found Twilio WhatsApp only checked its 15 MiB aggregate after buffering each file. Its downloader now caps each response stream to the remaining aggregate and rejects the next fetch at exhaustion. A 5 MiB x 3 regression confirms the fourth request is never made. WhatsApp media suite passed 8/8, Agents typecheck and `git diff --check` passed; no Twilio request was made.

### OpenRouter Decisions API response bound (2026-10-20)

- System One already bounded request contexts (Clef Flash by default, full Decider only for long-context overflow) but parsed successful decision bodies with unbounded `response.json()` and read unbounded HTTP error text. Success now caps at 1 MiB; error bodies cap at 8 KiB, with surfaced detail truncated to 200 characters. Both paths enforce streamed bytes regardless of Content-Length.
- Added regressions for streamed oversized success and error responses. Agent SDK decision tests passed 20 cases; SDK typecheck and `git diff --check` passed. The 290-file workspace run above predates these SDK changes. No live OpenRouter call was made.
- The adjacent project-to-project MCP client also parsed arbitrary tool results with `response.json()`. It now applies the same 1 MiB streamed cap and rejects malformed responses. A missing-Content-Length overflow regression passes. Agent SDK suite after both bounded-client changes passed 24 files / 111 tests; SDK typecheck and `git diff --check` passed. No live MCP request was made.
- The Artifact Repository SDK client read successful JSON and failed-response bodies without byte limits. Success bodies now cap at 16 MiB; error bodies cap at 8 KiB and exposed diagnostic text at 200 characters. Three tests cover success, streamed overflow without Content-Length and large error bodies. Agent SDK suite passed 25 files / 114 tests after all three client changes; SDK typecheck and `git diff --check` passed. No live Artifact Worker binding was exercised.

### Model provider response allocation bounds (2026-10-20)

- Continued response audit found the OpenRouter public catalog checked its 8 MiB cap only after `response.text()` allocated the entire body. It now enforces that limit while reading the stream. The universal model client accumulated both SSE and non-streaming provider replies without a total byte bound; it now caps either path at 16 MiB, checks actual bytes even without Content-Length, and fails oversized SSE instead of returning partial model output.
- Added oversized catalog, SSE, and non-streaming regressions. Catalog tests passed 6/6; the two new root-config stream-bound tests passed; Agents typecheck and `git diff --check` passed. The root reasoner-streaming fixtures were stale against the current planner contract (`answer`/`run` wrapper tools); changed them to plain-text answers and direct operation calls. The full root streaming file now passes 29/29. Biome still reports pre-existing diagnostics in these large legacy files; no unrelated whole-file rewrite was made. No live provider responses were exercised.
- Browser `see` previously buffered screenshot responses with `arrayBuffer()` before enforcing the 4 MiB image cap. It now reads incrementally and rejects declared or streamed overflow before retaining the full response. Browser screenshot tests passed 2/2; Agents typecheck and `git diff --check` passed. SDK and Agents typechecks also pass after adding the Workers-compatible TextDecoder `ignoreBOM` option to bounded decoders.
- The container virtual-display client likewise buffered its screenshot with `arrayBuffer()`. It now streams under a 16 MiB cap matching the container process's existing maxBuffer, with a missing-Content-Length overflow regression. Launchpad runtime tests passed 13/13; SDK typecheck and `git diff --check` passed.
- Durable extension artifact-set verification also fetched full object text before checking its 2 MiB aggregate. It now rejects from R2 listing size before `get()`, confirms listed and fetched sizes match, and checks actual UTF-8 bytes before digesting. Regression asserts the oversized object is never fetched; workflow scope suite passed 6/6, Agents typecheck and `git diff --check` passed.
- Completed the entire separate legacy root Vitest run: 655 test files (559 pass, 96 fail), 4,862 tests (4,474 pass, 388 fail), plus one unhandled error. Failures cluster around stale planner schemas, outdated route/auth fixtures, missing R2/DO bindings and SQLite migrations, changed worker API contracts, and one missing Shell import alias. This is a broad root-harness triage queue, not evidence that all failures are production bugs; verify each against the registered package suite/current interfaces before changing runtime code.
- New provider/workflow response review found unbounded `.text()` allocation in Twilio number search/pricing, generic subagent results, and AgentTaskWorkflow error parsing. Added a streaming byte-limited response reader (Twilio search 256 KiB, pricing 64 KiB, subagent result 2 MiB/error detail 8 KiB, task error 256 KiB); also bounded workflow agent-config hashing to 1 MiB per R2 file with a UTF-8 size consistency check. Focused suite passed 5 files / 20 tests, Agents TypeScript check and `git diff --check` passed. `pnpm --filter` itself was blocked by pnpm's ignored-build approval gate during its automatic install; direct local `tsc` succeeded. No live external calls were performed.
- Extended streamed caps to OpenRouter entitlement checks (64 KiB), Virtual CLI API replies (1 MiB success / 8 KiB errors), company automation results (1 MiB), and WorkflowRunDO effect JSON results (4 MiB). Added a Virtual CLI overflow regression and corrected two R2 test fixtures to provide required object sizes. Final Agents run passed 135 files / 567 tests; TypeScript check and shared-reader Biome check passed. Whole-file Biome checks on legacy touched files still show existing style/format issues. No live service bindings or deployment were exercised.
- Bound Agents ingress allocations: signed Twilio forms (16 KiB), connector/channel webhooks and extension callbacks (1 MiB), tunnel enrollment/auth (32 KiB), and every JSON request at the Worker entry point (4 MiB). Assistant inline R2 file reads now reject from head metadata before download; the shared CAS read accepts a preflight ceiling, and artifact preview success/error responses stream under 2 MiB / 8 KiB. Added request and R2 boundary regressions; Agents suite passed 136 files / 571 tests and typecheck passed.
- Router audit found transcription only checked its 10 MiB body cap after `formData()`/`arrayBuffer()`, and Desk Drop proxy copied arbitrary non-GET bodies using `arrayBuffer()`. Both now read incrementally under 10 MiB and 64 KiB ceilings; transcript output is capped at 256 KiB. Router transcription tests passed 3/3 and router typecheck passed. No provider/deployment calls were made.
- Added route-level Desk Drop regression: oversized body returns 413 without calling the Spaces Worker. Bounded-ingress plus transcription focus passed 4/4; full Router suite passed 8 files / 39 tests, Router typecheck passed, and new helper/test Biome checks passed.
- Added shared `@naelao/http` bounded text/JSON response readers and applied them to Cohere Parse (16 MiB), Tavily search/extract (4 MiB), Threads summaries (64 KiB), QueueHealth GraphQL (1 MiB), and workflow error details (8 KiB read / 300 characters retained). KB ingestion now size-checks its second R2 read and verifies UTF-8 byte length. HTTP tests passed 8 files / 27 tests; Knowledge passed 6 files / 25; HTTP package build/typecheck and Knowledge/Workflows/Threads typechecks passed. No live providers were called.
- Added a shared streaming request reader (Content-Length preflight plus streamed actual-byte limit, without cloning) and extended its tests. Artifact upload bodies cap at 7 MiB before parsing, with an oversized no-Content-Length integration regression proving no R2 write. Browser request JSON caps at 32 KiB; Spaces dynamic-page/gadget-MCP and raw task/document lease forwards cap at 1 MiB, with 413 mapping; Billing webhook bodies cap at 1 MiB; Channels webhook bodies cap at 1 MiB. Whop/Gumroad connector responses cap at 1 MiB; Billing payout-page, portal, eval and MCP upstream error details are bounded to 8 KiB of input and no more than 300 displayed characters. Focused shared-reader and Artifact regressions passed 4/4 and 1/1; Artifact suite passed 3 files / 10 tests; Spaces focused suite passed 2 files / 15 tests; all affected worker typechecks passed and `git diff --check` passed. This remains a partial audit; the historical root Vitest failures still need triage. No live providers or deployment were exercised.
